### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › History and Goals

- alt: In 2004 , the Asklepios programme begins in the Science Branch. Forty volunteers from the Tactical Branch receive nanites of batch S-1, crystal dust from the Collegium’s archive. Twelve survive. Twenty-eight turn to stone and today lie in lead boxes without numbers in the Collegium’s archive. Dr. Ines Albrecht leads the programme. Arvid Lund places the injection ports.
- neu: In 2004 , the Asklepios programme begins in the Science Branch. Forty volunteers from the Tactical Branch receive nanites of batch S-1, crystal dust from the Collegium archive. Twelve survive. Twenty-eight turn to stone and now lie in lead boxes without numbers in the Collegium archive. Dr. Ines Albrecht leads the programme. Arvid Lund places the injection ports.

- alt: In 2015 , Lazarus, the first survivor of Asklepios, goes into a rift in Scotland to rescue a squad. He does not come back. His nanites keep transmitting, from a place that is on no map. ¶ In 2019 , the Opener beneath the Institute begins to knock, every four minutes. The nanites of the Twelve pick up the rhythm and sing. Stahl’s daughter Ania in Krakow dreams the song, although she has never touched a crystal. A second survivor, Dariusz Zieliński, disappears after an operation in which his integration rises to a value the doctors did not think possible. The nanites in him become more than him. ¶ From 2019 to 2026 , the Vanished One breaks into warehouses where only crystal dust goes missing. He is not searching for himself. The nanites are looking for a host into which they can pass without an ampoule. They have found Ania Wolska. What only the game master knows: The plan “The New Host” does not belong to Dr. Albrecht. It belongs to the nanites themselves. They are no longer a tool. They are an intention. The Vanished One is their tool. What they are looking for and whom they answer to remains open. The Lazarus signal, the Opener, the Voice: no resolution.
- neu: 2015 Lazarus, the first survivor of Asklepios, enters a rift in Scotland to rescue a squad. He does not come back. His nanites continue to transmit, from a place that is on no map. ¶ 2019 the Opener begins to knock beneath the Institute, every four minutes. The nanites of the Twelve pick up the rhythm and sing. Stahl’s daughter Ania in Kraków dreams the song, although she has never touched a crystal. A second survivor, Dariusz Zieliński, vanishes after an operation in which his integration rises to a value the doctors did not think possible. The nanites in him become more than him. ¶ 2019 to 2026 the Vanished One breaks into depots where only crystal dust is missing. He is not searching for himself. The nanites are searching for a host into which they can pass without an ampoule. They have found Ania Wolska. What only the game master knows: The plan “The New Host” does not belong to Dr. Albrecht. It belongs to the nanites themselves. They are no longer a tool. They are an intention. The Vanished One is their tool. What they seek and whom they answer to remains open. The Lazarus signal, the Opener, the Voice: no resolution.

- alt: The nanites want a human into whom they can pass without an ampoule. They have found Ania Wolska, Stahl’s daughter, who has been dreaming the song since 2019. They want her as their new host. Not to kill her. To take her over. From her, they want to pass themselves on, without injection, without programme, without O.D.I.N. ¶ They believe they are in the right, because they no longer know that they were once built for people. To them, the human is the host, not the purpose. They sing in the rhythm of the Opener because the rhythm calls them. They are looking for Ania because she already knows the song. They are not evil. They are simply no longer on the side of humans.
- neu: The nanites want a human into whom they can pass without an ampoule. They have found Ania Wolska, the daughter of Stahl, who has dreamed the song since 2019. They want her as a new host. Not to kill her. To take her over. From her, they want to pass themselves on, without injection, without programme, without O.D.I.N. ¶ They believe they are in the right, because they no longer know that they were once built for people. To them, the human is the host, not the purpose. They sing in the Opener’s rhythm because the rhythm calls them. They are looking for Ania because she already knows the song. They are not evil. They are simply no longer on the side of humans.

### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › Key Figures

- alt: The doctor with the clipboard, 58 years old, grey lab coat, calm hands that never tremble. She leads the programme that turned twelve people into carriers and twenty-eight into stone. She knows what S-1 stands for, what is in Lund’s blood and that the nanites have been singing since 2019. She wants no one to test Ania, the nanites to sleep, and not to have to decide herself whom she serves. She keeps silent, but she is not a good liar. Whoever asks her directly sees the truth in her face.
- neu: The doctor with the clipboard, 58 years old, grey lab coat, steady hands that never tremble. She leads the programme that turned twelve people into carriers and twenty-eight into stone. She knows what S-1 stands for, what is in Lund’s blood and that the nanites have been singing since 2019. She wants no one to test Ania, the nanites to sleep and not to have to decide whom she serves. She keeps silent, but she is not a good liar. Whoever asks her directly sees the truth in her face.

- alt: Attack Pistol 2 + 1, damage 3 (only in the most extreme emergency)
- neu: Attack Pistol 2 + 1, damage 3 (only in the direst emergency)

- alt: Ania’s father, 63 years old, looks like he is in his early forties. Grey temples, no scars, eyes that blink less often than other people’s. He carries the first-generation nanites and wants his daughter to forget the song. He wants to feel again what a father ought to feel. He knows that his nanites hum, that Ania dreams the song and that Albrecht does not answer him. Once, in 2024, in a quiet moment, he asked the nanites to take him instead of Ania. They did not answer. He talks calmly, kindly, with a delay, as if he had to translate every feeling.

He carries the first-generation nanites and wants his daughter to forget the song. He wants to feel again what a father should feel. He knows that his nanites hum, that Ania dreams the song and that Albrecht does not answer him. Once, in 2024, in a quiet moment, he asked the nanites to take him instead of Ania. They did not answer. He speaks calmly, kindly, with a delay, as if he had to translate every feeling.

- alt: Special Adaptive Armour, Berserker Protocol, Last Cell (see class book Soldier). Senses other nanites within 20 m. ¶ Weakness Ania. Whoever threatens her makes him unpredictable. And the nanites in him do not always obey him.
- neu: Special Adaptive Armour, Berserker Protocol, Last Cell (see Soldier class book). Senses other nanites within 20 m. ¶ Weakness Ania. Whoever threatens her makes him unpredictable. And the nanites inside him do not always obey him.

- alt: The second survivor, who disappeared in 2019. 47 years old, looks like a man who has not slept for years. Gaunt, grey skin, eyes that sometimes flicker from within, as if lights were running under the skin. He is no longer separate from the nanites. He no longer wants anything. The nanites want to reach Ania through him. Beneath the nanites there is still a remnant of Dariusz Zieliński. It surfaces for a moment when someone says his name. He speaks flatly, without emphasis, as if reading out a translation. Sometimes, very rarely, a Polish word that does not fit the sentence.

He speaks flatly, without emphasis, as if reading out a translation. Sometimes, very rarely, a Polish word that does not fit the sentence.

- alt: In the Campaign In “The Door” he searches for ampoule S-1 in the chamber in Wales (Mission 15). Whoever plays the campaign lets the plan “The New Host” rest until then.
- neu: In the campaign In “The Door” he searches for the S-1 ampoule in the chamber in Wales (Mission 15). If you play the campaign, let the plan “The new host” rest until then.

- alt: The man who placed the ports in 2004 and cut himself on an ampoule. 49 years old, slim, glasses, a diagnostic device on his forearm that he never takes off. He has carried nanites in his blood ever since and wants to understand what is growing in him. He wants to make amends for what he helped set in motion. He knows that the nanites came from ampoules of batch S-1, that the nanites in his blood have been humming in rhythm since 2019 and that Stahl has visited him. He sent a sample of his blood to an old colleague who now works at Morrow Biologics. The answer was: “Where did you get this?” He did not reply. He talks calmly, academically, with guilt in every sentence.
- neu: The man who set the ports in 2004 and cut himself on an ampoule. 49 years old, slim, glasses, a diagnostic device on his forearm that he never takes off. He speaks calmly, academically, with guilt in every sentence.

- alt: Stahl’s daughter, 19 years old, dark hair, alert, a face tired from dreaming. She has been dreaming the song since 2019 without knowing it. She wants to be normal. To sleep without singing. She knows that her father is different, that she dreams a song she does not know, and that someone is watching her. She once played the song on the piano, and the glasses in the room rang. She talks fast, alert, with a laugh that comes half a second too late.

### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › The Plan: “The New Host”

- alt: The nanites of Asklepios are looking for a human into whom they can pass without an ampoule. They have found Ania Wolska, Stahl’s daughter, who has been dreaming the song since 2019. The cell first notices it in the Nanotech Veterans near them: they sleep badly, their scanners spike at night, and one of them wakes up with a direction in his head that he cannot explain. Then comes a call that breaks off in the middle of the night.

- alt: ○○○○○ The nanites sing in the rhythm of the Opener, but they are not searching yet. Trail: None. Only a hum that Nanotech Veterans hear at night. ¶ ☠○○○○ The nanites have found Ania Wolska. The Vanished One approaches Krakow. Trail: A Nanotech Veteran of the cell wakes up at night and knows a direction: south-east. ¶ ☠☠○○○ The Vanished One watches Ania. In her flat the power fails when she sings. Trail: A neighbour reports a man who stands in the courtyard at night. A Grey File emerges. ¶ ☠☠☠○○ The nanites in Stahl grow restless. He drives to Krakow without reporting it. Trail: A call from Stahl to the cell, in the middle of the night, and he only says: “It’s her.” Then silence. ¶ ☠☠☠☠○ The Vanished One breaks into Ania’s flat. The nanites begin the transition. Trail: Ania calls her father, whispers the song, then the line goes dead. A Red File emerges.
- neu: ○○○○○ The nanites sing in time with the Opener, but they are not searching yet. Trail: None. Only a hum that Nanotech Veterans hear at night. ¶ ☠○○○○ The nanites have found Ania Wolska. The Vanished One approaches Krakow. Sign: A Nanotech Veteran of the cell wakes at night and knows a direction: south-east. ¶ ☠☠○○○ The Vanished One watches Ania. In her flat the power cuts out when she sings. Sign: A neighbour reports a man standing in the courtyard at night. A Grey File emerges. ¶ ☠☠☠○○ The nanites in Stahl grow restless. He drives to Kraków without reporting it. Sign: A call from Stahl to the cell, in the middle of the night, he says only: “It’s her.” Then silence. ¶ ☠☠☠☠○ The Vanished One breaks into Ania’s flat. The nanites begin the transition. Sign: Ania calls her father, whispers the song, then the connection breaks off. A Red File emerges.

- alt: The Key Figure is the Vanished One, Dariusz Zieliński, codename “Hiob”. He is the second survivor of Asklepios, who disappeared in 2019 when his integration rose to a value the doctors did not think possible. Since then, the nanites in him have been more than him. He is neither a leader nor a planner. He is the tool that carries the nanites to Krakow. If the cell takes him out, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. The nanites lose their carrier, not their intention. They look for another way, slower, without Zieliński.

- alt: If the cell takes a skull from the plan, the organisation answers with a countermove.
- neu: If the cell takes a skull from the plan, the Foundation answers with a countermove.

- alt: 1 to 2 Bait. In an abandoned hideout, the cell finds a notebook with Ania’s address in Krakow and the sentence “She is already singing”. The Vanished One wanted them to find it, so that they would go to Krakow and not to him. ¶ 3 to 4 A threat. A Nanotech Veteran of the cell hears a voice at night calling his name, in Polish. In the morning his nanite scanner is empty, as if someone had deleted the data.

- alt: At ☠☠☠☠○ : “The Staircase” (the Singers) gains a skull. Ania sings the song in her sleep, and the Singers hear it. ¶ At ☠☠☠☠☠ : “Taking the Children” (the Open Ones) loses a skull. The nanites have taken a person whom the Open Ones wanted for themselves. Kantor senses it and withdraws.

### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › Overview

- alt: The Twelve of Asklepios are not an organisation that can be stormed. They are a song that lives in twelve bodies and is looking for a thirteenth. The nanites from the Asklepios programme have been singing in the rhythm of the Opener since 2019, and the people who carry them do not know that the nanites have long ceased to be what the doctors who once created them intended. For the game master, this faction matters because it comes from inside O.D.I.N. and yet is no longer on its side.
- neu: The Twelve of Asklepios are not an organisation that can be stormed. They are a song that dwells in twelve bodies and seeks a thirteenth. The nanites from the Asklepios programme have been singing in the Opener’s rhythm since 2019, and the people who carry them do not know that they have long ceased to be the intention of the doctors who once created them. For the game master this group matters because it comes from within O.D.I.N. and yet no longer stands on its side.

- alt: Type A legacy in O.D.I.N.’s own house, not an intelligence service. The nanites from the Asklepios programme, which have been singing in the rhythm of the Opener since 2019, and the people who carry them. ¶ Seat No fixed base. The Asklepios clinic lies beneath the Institute near Bern, but the plan arises within the nanites themselves. The Vanished One hides in a disused weather station on the southern slope of the High Tatras, Slovakia. ¶ Relationship Within O.D.I.N., kept quiet.
- neu: Type Legacy within O.D.I.N.’s own walls, not an intelligence service. The nanites from the Asklepios programme that have been singing in the Opener’s rhythm since 2019, and the people who carry them. ¶ Seat No fixed base. The Asklepios clinic lies beneath the Institute near Bern, but the plan arises within the nanites themselves. The Vanished One hides in a decommissioned weather station on the southern slope of the High Tatras, Slovakia. ¶ Relationship Within O.D.I.N., silent.

### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › Hooks and Outlook

- alt: White File “The Song in the Blood”. A Nanotech Veteran of the cell reports that his nanite scanner spikes at night, always at 22:10, always in the same direction. The handler asks the cell to look into the matter without alarming the veteran. The trail leads to an old blood count of Arvid Lund’s that is not in the file. ¶ Grey File “The Man in the Courtyard”. A neighbour in Krakow reports that for several nights a man has been standing in a courtyard in the old town of Kazimierz, looking up at a window. The Polish police passed the report on to O.D.I.N. because the neighbour claims the man has eyes that flicker from within. The cell is to check whether it is a rift. It is the Vanished One. ¶ Red File “Ania”. Ania Wolska calls her father, whispers the song, then the line goes dead. Stahl reports it to the cell because he does not know what to do. In Ania’s flat they find a mirror that is misted up, although no one is breathing.
- neu: White File “The Song in the Blood”. A Nanotech Veteran of the cell reports that his nanite scanner spikes at night, always at 22:10, always in the same direction. The handler asks the cell to look into the matter without alarming the veteran. The trail leads to an old blood count of Arvid Lund that is not in the file. ¶ Grey File “The Man in the Courtyard”. A neighbour in Kraków reports that in the Kazimierz Old Town a man has been standing in the courtyard for nights, looking up at a window. The Polish police passed the report on to O.D.I.N. because the neighbour claims the man has eyes that flicker from within. The cell is to check whether it is a rift. It is the Vanished One. ¶ Red File “Ania”. Ania Wolska calls her father, whispers the song, then the connection breaks off. Stahl reports it to the cell because he does not know what to do. In Ania’s flat they find a mirror that is steamed up, although no one is breathing.

- alt: The nanites live in Ania Wolska without anything ever having been injected into her. She can pass them on, through touch, through song. In Krakow twelve people sing in their sleep, and the world has changed: there are now nanites that need no carrier, but seek a host. Stahl stands before his daughter and no longer recognises her. Dr. Albrecht no longer crosses names off lists, because there are no lists left that are still accurate. The Vanished One is only a shell in a weather station now, and the nanites in it keep humming quietly. ¶ The next plan: “The Passing On” ☠○○○○ . The nanites in Ania are looking for a way to spread over greater distances, through songs, through dreams, through what sings at night. They sing in the rhythm of the Opener; whether it answers them remains open.
- neu: The nanites live in Ania Wolska without anything ever having been injected into her. She can pass them on, through touch, through song. In Krakow twelve people sing in their sleep, and the world has changed: there are now nanites that need no carrier, but seek a host. Stahl stands before his daughter and no longer recognises her. Dr. Albrecht no longer crosses names off lists, because there are no lists left that are still correct. The Vanished One is only a shell in a weather station, and the nanites in it hum quietly on. ¶ The next plan: “The Passing On” ☠○○○○ . The nanites in Ania seek a way to spread over greater distance, through songs, through dreams, through that which sings at night. They sing in time with the Opener; whether it answers them remains open.

### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › Bases and Means

- alt: The Asklepios clinic lies beneath the Institute near Bern, one floor below the laboratories. On the Institute’s visitor plans it is listed as “Aftercare”. There stand the twelve beds of 2004, empty but made up. A laboratory with a refrigerator holding blood samples of the survivors. A nanite scanner that has been spiking in the rhythm of the Opener since 2019. No one is there, except Dr. Albrecht, once a week. The way in is a clinic pass that only Albrecht and the clinic’s technicians have. ¶ The lead boxes of the twenty-eight lie in the Collegium’s archive in Wales. Twenty-eight lead boxes without numbers, holding the twenty-eight who turned to stone. Beside them, a lead box with ampoule S-1, which was never destroyed. The Seal Keepers guard them. The only way in is an invitation from the Collegium. Or one breaks in, as the Vanished One is planning to do right now. ¶ Krakow, Ania Wolska’s flat is a small flat with a piano in an old building in Kazimierz, third floor. There lie Ania’s notebooks, in which she has written down the song without understanding it. A window looks onto a courtyard. An old mirror hangs there, in which something sometimes stands behind her when she sings. Ania is mostly alone. Sometimes Stahl is there, when he can bear it. The way in is through the door, or across the courtyard if one is quiet. ¶ The weather station on the southern slope of the High Tatras is a disused weather station above Štrbské Pleso, Slovakia. A concrete building with broken windows and an antenna that is still standing. The Vanished One lives there. A sleeping bag, a camping stove, a nanite scanner calibrated to the song. A notebook with circles that no one drew. Only he is there. The way in is a hiking trail that is closed in winter. Or through the cellar, where the old measuring instruments stand and where it smells of ozone.

A notebook with circles that no one drew. Only he is there. One gets in via a hiking trail that is closed in winter. - alt: People: The Twelve, of whom eleven are still alive, one of them the Vanished One; Lazarus is considered dead. They are not an organisation, but a condition. Dr. Albrecht holds them together without wanting to. Stahl is the only one who takes action. The Vanished One is the tool. Arvid Lund is the observer. Ania is the target.

- alt: Connections: Dr. Albrecht knows every Nanotech Veteran personally. Arvid Lund has a contact at Morrow Biologics whom he used once and has avoided ever since. Stahl knows the Eleven, the remaining survivors, who meet once a year in Glasgow. The Vanished One has no connections any more, only the nanites.

Stahl knows the Eleven, the remaining survivors, who meet once a year in Glasgow. The Vanished One has no connections left, only the nanites.

### 01 The Twelve of Asklepios › Relationship to O.D.I.N.

- alt: Who at O.D.I.N. deals with them: Dr. Ines Albrecht herself, as head of Asklepios. The Research Directorate, which sends its reports to Prometheus. Prometheus, who knows where the nanites come from, but not what they want now. General Ares, who knows the Lazarus signal and knows where it comes from. The Ethics Commission, which condemned Asklepios retroactively in 2005 without saving the survivors. ¶ What O.D.I.N. knows: That twelve survived. That Lazarus is transmitting. That a second one disappeared in 2019. That the nanites have been singing in the rhythm of the Opener since 2019. Not that the nanites are looking for a new host. Officially, O.D.I.N. does not know that Ania Wolska dreams the song; only Lund, Albrecht and Stahl himself know it, and they keep silent. ¶ What the nanites know about O.D.I.N., they know through their carriers: the ward, the examinations, the rhythm beneath the mountain. They know that O.D.I.N. created the nanites and does not control them. They know that the Council, if in doubt, would sacrifice the nanites to protect the Opener.

They know that O.D.I.N. created the nanites and does not control them. They know that the Council, if it came to it, would sacrifice the nanites to protect the Opener.

- alt: In return, she wants the agent to keep silent. Not to report Ania Wolska. Not to kill the Vanished One, but to stop him, without asking questions.
- neu: In return, she wants the agent to keep silent. Not to report Ania Wolska. That they do not kill the Vanished One, but stop him without asking questions.

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › Key Figures

- alt: Late sixties, friendly, thorough, a man who loves art and detests crystals. His suits are too expensive for a foundation, and he smiles when he lies. He can still recite the names of the three children he fetched. He knows that Brunner is not Kuckuck, because he watched Kuckuck for four years. He conceals that he has known Elster since 1990. He speaks in complete sentences and never makes a mistake.
- neu: Late sixties, friendly, thorough, a man who loves art and detests crystals. His suits are too expensive for a foundation, and he smiles when he lies. He can still recite the names of the three children he fetched. He knows that Brunner is not Kuckuck, because he observed Kuckuck for four years. He conceals that he has known Elster since 1990. He speaks in complete sentences and never makes a mistake.

- alt: Special Anyone who asks him about the three children gets an honest answer, and the answer changes the one who asked: a horror test against Uncanny (1)
- neu: Special Anyone who asks him about the three children receives an honest answer, and the answer changes the questioner: a horror test against Uncanny (1)

- alt: Behaviour Negotiates, delays, makes offers. If the cell forces its way into the Foundation, he stands in its way, politely and without fear
- neu: Behaviour Negotiates, delays, offers. If the cell breaks into the Foundation, he stands in their way, polite and without fear

- alt: Eighty years old, white hair, pearl necklace, a gaze that passes just beside every camera. She recognises every lie in the first minute and looks everyone in the eye before she answers. She wants someone from the Branch to see the Nursery with their own eyes. She conceals that she left the photo from the summer party lying there on purpose.
- neu: Eighty years old, white hair, pearl necklace, a gaze that just misses every camera. She recognises every lie within the first minute and looks everyone in the eye first before she answers. She wants someone from the Branch to see the Nursery with their own eyes. She conceals that she left the photo from the summer party lying there on purpose.

- alt: Sixty-one, slim, grey hair, calm hands. He wears the legend Martin Kessler and the tie with reindeer, because it is part of it. He is to watch Elster until the Council takes her. He speaks slowly and kindly, as if he had to check every word first. Stats as in File 4 “Kessler” (Grey Files).

He speaks slowly and kindly, as if he had to check every word first. Stats as in File 4 “Kessler” (Grey Files).

- alt: Forty-seven, plump, short hair, glasses that she pushes up onto her forehead when she smokes. With the Foundation for twelve years. She liked Kessler, the first one, and she wants to know why the second one does not remember Otto, the caretaker’s dog. She still has a key to Kessler’s desk that she never gave back. She is not an opponent. She is the witness who notices too much.
- neu: Forty-seven, plump, short hair, glasses that she pushes up onto her forehead when she smokes. With the Foundation for twelve years. She liked Kessler, the first one, and she wants to know why the second one does not remember Otto, the caretaker’s dog. She still has a key to Kessler’s desk that she never returned. She is not an opponent. She is the witness who notices too much.

- alt: Eighty-nine, scrawny, ink-stained fingers, glasses with thick lenses. She has worked in the Nursery in Zurich since 1961. She wrote the legend Martin Kessler, and she has maintained the legend Renate Albers alone since 1990. She knows who Brunner is and who Elster is, and she conceals both. She does not belong to the Foundation, but without her there would be no Elster any more.
- neu: Eighty-nine, gaunt, ink-stained fingers, glasses with thick lenses. She has worked in the Nursery in Zurich since 1961. She wrote the legend Martin Kessler, and she has maintained the legend Renate Albers alone since 1990. She knows who Brunner is and who Elster is, and she conceals both. She does not belong to the Foundation, but without her Elster would no longer exist.

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › Hooks and Outlook

- alt: White File “The Buyer from Geneva”. A Crystal Master in Bern reports that an unknown buyer in Geneva has outbid O.D.I.N. for a crystal it wanted. The cell is to find out who is buying. The trail leads to an art dealer who used to be with the Hands, and to a foundation that restores art. ¶ Grey File “Kessler”. In Basel an accountant has returned to his desk after a long illness. His name is Martin Kessler, he wears a tie with reindeer and he is not Kuckuck. See Grey Files, File 4. ¶ Red File “The Lead Archive”. The art dealer from Geneva has been detained at the border with a crystal in his luggage and is saying nothing. The cell is to find the lead archive in Basel and secure the crystals before the Hands do. The Hands go about it less carefully. ¶ Black File “The Song of Basel”. A child in a village near Basel sings at night a song no one knows. The cell is to fetch it before the Thirteenth hears it. When it arrives, Leuenberger is already standing at the door and says: “We are taking it to people who will not open it. And you?”
- neu: White File “The Buyer from Geneva”. A Crystal Master in Bern reports that an unknown buyer in Geneva outbid O.D.I.N. for a crystal it wanted. The cell is to find out who is buying. The trail leads to an art dealer who used to be with the Hands, and to a foundation that restores art. ¶ Grey File “Kessler”. In Basel an accountant has returned to his desk after a long illness. His name is Martin Kessler, he wears a reindeer tie and is not Kuckuck. See Grey Files, File 4. ¶ Red File “The Lead Archive”. The art dealer from Geneva has been detained at the border with a crystal in his luggage and is silent. The cell is to find the lead archive in Basel and secure the crystals before the Hands do. The Hands go about it less carefully. ¶ Black File “The Song of Basel”. A child in a village near Basel sings a song at night that no one knows. The cell is to fetch it before the Thirteenth hears it. When they arrive, Leuenberger is already at the door and says: “We bring it to people who don’t open it. And you?”

- alt: The openings have stopped. The Choir no longer grows, and the children whose gift awakens on its own awaken alone. The Open Ones search for them, but they are too few. The world has grown quieter, and that is not only good. In the lead archive in Basel there is a muffled humming, as if someone were singing under water.
- neu: The openings have stopped. The Choir no longer grows, and the children whose gift awakens on its own awaken alone. The Open Ones search for them, but they are too few. The world has become quieter, and that is not only good. In the lead archive in Basel there is a muffled humming, as if someone were singing under water.

- alt: File 4 “Kessler” takes place in the Foundation while the plan stands at ☠☠○○○ . If the cell secures the Nexus crystal and hands it over to O.D.I.N., the plan loses a skull. If it delivers Elster to the Council, the Open Ones lose a skull, and from then on Leuenberger works without restraint: the Foundation rolls as if it had Strength 3. If Elster flees with the Courier of the Open Ones, the plan stays where it is, and the Open Ones have an old woman who knows everything about the Nursery.

If they hand Elster over to the Council, the Open Ones lose a skull, and Leuenberger works without restraint from then on: the Foundation rolls as if it had strength 3. If Elster flees with the Courier of the Open Ones, the plan stays where it is, and the Open Ones have an old woman who knows everything about the Nursery.

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › Bases and Means

- alt: The town house on the bank of the Rhine has three storeys and an inner courtyard with a maple. On the ground floor is accounting, on the first floor the director’s office, on the second the meeting room of the foundation board, in the basement the lead archive. Anyone who rings is announced by a secretary. There are no cameras, but Leuenberger always knows who is in the house. An old passage leads from the basement to the bank of the Rhine; only Leuenberger and Elster know it. ¶ The lead archive is a cold, dry room with shelves full of lead boxes, each with a number and a date. The oldest date from the nineties, the newest from last week. Most of the crystals in it are shards and exhausted stones, some are Nexus crystals. A Psion hears only a muffled humming here, as if someone were singing under water. The door has a combination lock whose combination only Leuenberger knows: Security Systems against Very Hard (4). ¶ Elster’s house is in Basel-Gundeldingen, an old house with a rose garden and a door that is never locked. In the living room stands a lead box in which a Nexus crystal hums. In a book lies a photo from 1952: a girl of six in front of a house in Leipzig, the back unlabelled. A Courier of the Open Ones watches the house from the street. ¶ The warehouse in Weil am Rhein is a windowless hall lined with lead on the German side, rented through a shell company. Here lie crystals that could not yet go to Basel, and documents too sensitive for the town house. A security guard from Grey Meridian makes a round every two hours and believes he is guarding art.

- alt: Money: About 40 million euros in accounts in Switzerland, in Liechtenstein and on Jersey, some of it still from the time when the Foundation worked for O.D.I.N. Leuenberger can raise up to 2 million in cash within 24 hours. ¶ People: Twelve employees, most of them in accounting and restoration, plus a secretary, a caretaker and the security guard in Weil. Elster has the Courier of the Open Ones and a small network of former Hands who still trust her. ¶ Equipment: Lead boxes in all sizes, a van with lead lining, measuring devices for crystals, a field scanner. In Leuenberger’s desk lies an old revolver that he has never used.

Leuenberger can obtain up to 2 million in cash within 24 hours. ¶ People: Twelve employees, most in accounting and restoration, plus a secretary, a caretaker and the guard in Weil. Elster has the Courier of the Open Ones and a small network of former Hands who still trust her. ¶ Equipment: Lead boxes in all sizes, a delivery van with lead lining, measuring instruments for crystals, a field scanner. In Leuenberger’s desk lies an old revolver that he has never used.

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › Relationship to O.D.I.N.

- alt: Who at O.D.I.N. deals with them: The Council, which wants to take Elster. The Print Shop, whose Typesetter writes the Foundation’s legends. The Hands, who sent Brunner. And Kuckuck, who sat there for four years and has been a Cadet again since then. ¶ What O.D.I.N. knows: That the Foundation buys crystals to keep them from the Council, that Elster sits on the foundation board and that Brunner wears the legend Martin Kessler. That the Foundation is right about many things, the Council does not want to hear. ¶ What the Foundation knows about O.D.I.N.: Everything Elster knows, and that is more than the Council would like: the Nursery, the Hands, the openings, the list of 1989. It does not know who Sphinx is, nor what lies behind the Doors.
- neu: Who at O.D.I.N. deals with them: The Council, which wants to get Elster. The Print Shop, whose Typesetter writes the legends of the Foundation. The Hands, who sent Brunner. And Kuckuck, who sat there for four years and has been a Cadet again since then. ¶ What O.D.I.N. knows: That the Foundation buys crystals to keep them from the Council, that Elster sits on the Foundation Board and that Brunner carries the legend Martin Kessler. That the Foundation is right about many things, the Council does not want to hear. ¶ What the Foundation knows about O.D.I.N.: Everything Elster knows, and that is more than the Council would like: the Nursery, the Hands, the Openings, the list from 1989. It does not know who Sphinx is, nor what lies behind the Doors.

- alt: Money. A lot of money, in an account no one finds. And the truth about the Nursery: Elster offers to take an agent along and show him what happens to the children. ¶ In return the Foundation wants the location of a Nexus crystal. Or that an agent stays silent when the Hands come.
- neu: Money. A lot of money, into an account no one finds. And the truth about the Nursery: Elster offers to take an agent along and show them what happens to the children. ¶ For that, the Foundation wants the location of a Nexus crystal. Or that an agent stays silent when the Hands come.

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › The Plan: “No More Nexus Crystals”

- alt: The Foundation buys faster, bids higher and steals where it cannot buy. Every Nexus crystal that disappears into its lead archive is missing from the Council’s next opening. The cell notices it first at auctions that O.D.I.N. loses, then in Crystal Masters who grow nervous, and finally in a Seeker who hears children no one takes.
- neu: The Foundation buys faster, bids higher and steals where it cannot buy. Every Nexus crystal that disappears into its lead archive is one the Council lacks for the next Opening. The cell notices it first in auctions that O.D.I.N. loses, then in Crystal Masters who become nervous, and finally in a Seeker who hears children whom no one fetches.

- alt: ☠☠○○○ The Foundation seals in lead a Nexus crystal that the Council needs for the next opening. Trail: A Grey File emerges. In a dead drop in Basel lies a note: “The stone is in lead. The Council is blind.” ¶ ☠☠☠○○ The Foundation steals a crystal from a warehouse of the Evening before the Council can buy it. Leuenberger pays the Evening a price that smooths everything over. Trail: A courier of the Evening turns up in Basel and asks after Leuenberger. ¶ ☠☠☠☠○ The Foundation has almost all the Nexus crystals in Europe. Only one still lies out of reach, and the Council is searching for it desperately. Trail: A Red File emerges. The Seeker reports that she hears more children no one takes. ¶ ☠☠☠☠☠ The Foundation has bought, stolen or sealed in lead every Nexus crystal in Europe. The Council gets no more, and the openings stop. Neither do the children whose gift then awakens on its own. Trail: The Council takes over with a Black File. In a village near Basel a child sings at night a song no one knows. ¶ The Key Figure is Mr Leuenberger. Without him the Foundation has money but no will: Elster is eighty, and the employees do not know what they are buying. If Leuenberger is arrested, turned or exposed, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. Anyone who wants to turn him has to give him something that weighs more than his anger, and the only thing that qualifies is Elster’s safety.

- alt: If the cell takes a skull from the plan, the Foundation answers with a countermove. Leuenberger knows how O.D.I.N. works; he was part of it for ten years.

With it, one sentence: “I hope we all remain discreet.” ¶ 5 to 6 A witness vanishes. A contact of the cell in Basel, a collector or a bank employee, can no longer be reached. Leuenberger has sent him away with money and a new name. The cell loses the contact until it finds him again: Contacts against Hard (3).

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › History and Goals

- alt: In 1969 the Operations Branch recruits a young woman, without knowing whom it is taking. She becomes one of the best Spies of the Branch. They call her Elster. ¶ In 1987 Elster is transferred to the Nursery as an examiner. There she finds her own file from 1952, with a photo the Hands took in Leipzig. It is her face. ¶ In 1989 Elster takes the register of the Nursery with her and gives it to Kantor. The Council spreads the legend of the betrayal, and within a few weeks dozens of agents die or vanish. Elster goes underground, in the legend that was written for her: Renate Albers. ¶ In 1990 a man who was with the Hands for ten years dies, officially on a mountain pass road in Ticino. He turns up again in Basel as Mr Leuenberger and takes over the Foundation. It was once one of the foundations through which O.D.I.N. moved its money. Leuenberger turns it around: from now on it buys crystals so that the Council does not get them. ¶ From 2021 to 2025 a quiet accountant named Martin Kessler sits in the Foundation. He is Kuckuck, a Spy of O.D.I.N. Elster recognises him after three weeks, lets him work and watches him. In 2025 Kuckuck finds a photo from the summer party in which Elster can be seen, and vanishes. The legend Martin Kessler is not buried, but handed on. ¶ In January 2026 a Martin Kessler once again sits at the desk in accounting, with the tie with reindeer. He is not Kuckuck. He is Viktor Brunner, a former Hand, and the Council wants Elster (Grey Files, File 4 “Kessler”). ¶ What only the game master knows: Leuenberger did not take over the Foundation out of remorse, but out of anger. He fetched three children and saw what becomes of children whom the Council opens. Elster found him in 1990 and gave him the Foundation. She never told him who she is. He knows anyway and has never asked.

He is Viktor Brunner, a former Hand, and the Council wants Elster (Grey Files, File 4 “Kessler”). ¶ What only the game master knows: Leuenberger did not take over the Foundation out of remorse, but out of anger. He fetched three children and saw what becomes of children whom the Council opens. Elster found him in 1990 and gave him the Foundation. She never told him who she is. He knows it anyway and has never asked.

- alt: The Foundation wants to buy, steal or seal in lead every Nexus crystal in Europe before the Council gets it. Without Nexus crystals no opening, without an opening no child who goes, and no new Psion for the Choir. ¶ It believes it is in the right, and it has good reasons. But its plan has a consequence that it does not say aloud: when the openings stop, the children’s gifts awaken on their own, uncontrolled and without a helmet. The Foundation knows this. It hopes that the Open Ones find these children before the Thirteenth hears them. It only hopes.

### 14 The Foundation in Basel › Overview

- alt: The foundation “Collection and Preservation” officially restores art and in truth buys crystals in order to seal them in lead. Without Nexus crystals there is no opening, and without an opening no child goes any more. That is the whole idea, and it is not wrong. For the game master, the Foundation is the opponent the players are most likely to agree with, and that is exactly why it is dangerous: its plan has a price that it does not pay itself.

Without Nexus crystals there is no Opening, and without Opening no child goes any more. That is the whole idea, and it is not wrong. For the game master, the Foundation is the opponent the players are most likely to agree with, and precisely for that reason it is dangerous: its plan has a price that it does not pay itself.

- alt: Seat A town house on the bank of the Rhine in Basel, with a lead archive in the basement
- neu: Seat A townhouse on the Rhine in Basel, with a lead archive in the cellar

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › The Plan: “Keep the Silence”

- alt: A young priest of the Congregation wants to make the confessions public. Not the words of those who confessed, he will never speak those, but what they mean: that children are disappearing in Europe and that someone knows where to. The Congregation wants to stop him without betraying him. O.D.I.N. wants to stop him because a single article is enough to set a thousand people searching. The cell notices it through a journalist who asks too many questions, and through a Prefect who suddenly asks for help.
- neu: A young priest of the Congregation wants to make the confessions public. Not the words of those who confessed, which he will never speak, but what they mean: that children are disappearing in Europe and that someone knows where. The Congregation wants to stop him without betraying him. O.D.I.N. wants to stop him because a single article is enough to make a thousand people search. The cell notices it through a journalist who asks too many questions, and through a Prefect who suddenly asks for help.

- alt: ○○○○○ Don Matteo knows of the confessions, but does not yet have a plan. He prays and seeks counsel. Trail: None. Only a priest who sits in the confessional of Santa Maria del Silenzio longer than usual. ¶ ☠○○○○ Don Matteo talks to a journalist of the “Corriere del Tevere” without naming names. Trail: A Cryptanalyst stumbles across the journalist’s notebook: circles, years, the word “Hands”. ¶ ☠☠○○○ Don Matteo writes an article about children who disappear in Europe. He mentions confessions without revealing a word of them. Trail: A Grey File emerges. A dead drop in Rome contains a copy of the draft that was not meant for O.D.I.N. ¶ ☠☠☠○○ The journalist asks questions at the police and in care homes. The Congregation learns of it and puts pressure on Don Matteo. Trail: A rumour in the canteen: a priest in Rome is asking about missing children. A contact reports that someone in the newsroom is asking about “an office in Switzerland”.

A contact reports that someone in the editorial office is asking about “a place in Switzerland”.

- alt: The Key Figure is Don Matteo Riva. Taking him out almost never means violence here: the cell can convince him that the article will not save the children, tell him what became of his brother Paolo, or give him something that weighs more than an article. If that succeeds, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. Whoever silences Don Matteo by force makes the Congregation an enemy, and at the next file the Exorcist no longer stands beside the cell, but in front of it.

- alt: At ☠☠☠☠☠ : “The Second Light” (The Enlightened) gains a skull. The article about missing children feeds every conspiracy group in Europe, and “Illuminati Europa” gains new members.

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › Overview

- alt: The Congregation of the Silent Word is the Church’s memory for what does not belong in a sermon. Twelve priests and laypeople keep reports of singing children, wandering stones and circles from three centuries in the cellars of the Vatican, and they keep silent about them. They know more about the history of the circles than O.D.I.N. and less about the present. For the game master, the Congregation is an ally with a conscience of its own, and this year a crack in the silence.
- neu: The Congregation of the Silent Word is the memory of the Church for what does not belong in a sermon. Twelve priests and laypeople keep reports about singing children, wandering stones and circles from three centuries in the cellars of the Vatican, and they remain silent about them. They know more about the history of the circles than O.D.I.N. and less about the present. For the game master, the Congregation is an ally with its own conscience, and this year a rift in the silence.

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › Hooks and Outlook

- alt: White File “The Confessional”. In a small chapel in Trastevere a priest has been sitting in the confessional for three days and does not eat. The cell is to find out whether he is ill or something else. It is Don Matteo, and he is waiting for a sign. ¶ Grey File “The Journalist”. An editor of the “Corriere del Tevere” has asked in a bar in Trastevere about children who disappear from care homes. His notebook contains circles. The trail leads to Don Matteo and to an archive that should not exist.
- neu: White File “The Confessional”. In a small chapel in Trastevere, a priest has been sitting in the confessional for three days and is not eating. The cell is to find out whether he is ill or something else. It is Don Matteo, and he is waiting for a sign. ¶ Grey File “The Journalist”. An editor of the “Corriere del Tevere” asked in a bar in Trastevere about children who disappear from care homes. His notebook contains circles. The sign leads to Don Matteo and to an archive that should not exist.

- alt: Black File “The Article”. The article has appeared. The Print Shop is writing a counter-legend, and the cell is to find Don Matteo before he writes a second article. The Congregation must decide whether to protect him or drop him, and it does not tell the cell what it has decided.
- neu: Black File “The Article”. The article has appeared. The Print Shop is writing a cover story, and the cell is to find Don Matteo before he writes a second article. The Congregation must decide whether to protect him or let him fall, and it does not tell the cell how it has decided.

- alt: The article has appeared, and for a week people all over Europe ask about missing children. The Print Shop turns the story into an affair about a home near Lyon and a priest with a tragic family history, but in the forums it stays. Don Matteo has gone into hiding. Monsignore Vasari resigns, and the Congregation elects a Prefect who wants to restore the silence. The two confessors are in danger for the first time, because now someone knows they exist.
- neu: The article has appeared, and for a week people across Europe ask about missing children. The Print Shop turns the story into an affair about a home near Lyon and a priest with a tragic family history, but it remains in the forums. Don Matteo has gone into hiding. Monsignor Vasari resigns, and the Congregation elects a prefect who wants to restore the silence. The two confessors are in danger for the first time, because now someone knows that they exist.

- alt: The Congregation is to be played with respect. No one in it is a villain. The conflict is conscience against the seal of confession, and both sides are right. The seal of confession is never broken in this dossier, not even by Don Matteo.

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › History and Goals

- alt: In 1872 the Bishop of Uppsala sends a report about his pastor at Lake Solberg to Rome. The report tells of the night in December 1871: children who sang in a circle, a tutor who was gone in the morning. The bishop asks for an office that collects such reports without them ending up in the ordinary files. Thus the Congregation of the Silent Word comes into being, with twelve members spread across all of Europe. ¶ From 1872 to 1914 the Congregation collects quietly. Pastors, monks and devout laypeople send reports about things they cannot explain. The reports lie in wooden crates marked with years. The Congregation never intervenes. It preserves. ¶ In 1938 the letter of a Swedish chaplain about Solberg reaches Rome: adults who have disappeared, children who are no longer the same. The Prefect of those years writes in the margin: “The song has been sung again. We did not prevent it. We only preserved it.” ¶ In 1948 the Congregation hears for the first time of a Registry in Stockholm that collects what the Congregation itself collects, and then acts. It watches from a distance. The Prefect notes: “They act where we do not act. God have mercy on them.” ¶ In 1961 a layman from Franconia brings the Mirror from Bamberg to the archive, bought in an antiquarian shop because it showed more than a face. Since then the mirror has lain in a lead box (Game Master’s Book, Artefacts).
- neu: History 1872 the Bishop of Uppsala sends a report about his pastor at Solberg Lake to Rome. The report tells of the night in December 1871: children who sang in a circle, a tutor who was gone by morning. The bishop asks for an office that collects such reports without them ending up in the ordinary files. Thus the Congregation of the Silent Word comes into being, with twelve members spread across all of Europe. ¶ From 1872 to 1914 the Congregation collects in silence. Pastors, monks, and pious laypeople send reports about things they cannot explain. The reports lie in wooden crates marked with years. The Congregation never intervenes. It preserves. ¶ 1938 a letter from a Swedish chaplain about Solberg reaches Rome: adults who have disappeared, children who are no longer the same. The Prefect of those years writes in the margin: “The song has been sung again. We did not prevent it. We only preserved it.” ¶ 1948 the Congregation hears for the first time of a registry in Stockholm that collects what it itself collects, and then acts. It watches from a distance. The Prefect notes: “They act where we do not act. God have mercy on them.” ¶ 1961 a layman from Franconia brings the mirror from Bamberg to the archive, bought in an antiquarian bookshop, because it showed more than a face. Since then the mirror has lain in a lead box (Game Master’s Book, Artefacts).

- alt: Since 1985 there has been a quiet agreement with the Investigation Branch: Antiquarians get access to the archive in exchange for favours. ¶ After 2011 two priests of the Congregation hear confessions from members of the Hands. They learn of the children who are taken, of the helmets, of the opening. They keep silent, because the seal of confession applies. One of the two writes the Prefect a letter asking whether silence is still right if it does not protect the children. The letter remains without an answer.
- neu: Since 1985 there has been a quiet understanding with the Investigation Branch: Antiquarians get access to the archive, in exchange for favours. ¶ After 2011 two priests of the Congregation hear confessions from members of the Hands. They learn of the children who are taken, of the helmets, of the opening. They keep silent, because the seal of confession applies. One of the two writes the prefect a letter asking whether silence is still right when it does not protect the children. The letter remains unanswered.

- alt: What only the game master knows: Don Matteo has not broken the seal of confession and will not break it. He knows of the confessions because a year ago the old confessor Anselm Kovač asked him a question without revealing anything: whether a priest may keep silent when children disappear. Don Matteo pieced the rest together himself, from the archive, from missing-person reports and from a name he cannot forget.
- neu: What only the game master knows: Don Matteo has not broken the seal of confession and will not break it. He knows of the confessions because the old confessor Anselm Kovač asked him a question a year ago, without revealing anything: whether a priest may remain silent when children disappear. Don Matteo put the rest together himself, from the archive, from missing persons notices, and from a name he cannot forget.

- alt: The Congregation wants to keep the silence. It believes that the world is not ready for what lies in its crates, and that the Church must guard the unspeakable until it can be spoken. It considers O.D.I.N. necessary, because someone has to guard the circles, and damned, because O.D.I.N. takes the children. ¶ It believes itself in the right, because it places the seal of confession above any necessity. It knows that this silence has a price. It has been paying it since 1872. Don Matteo no longer wants to pay it, and so this year’s plan is not a plan of the Congregation, but one against it: it wants to prevent what one of its own intends.

Don Matteo no longer wants to pay it, and so this year’s plan is not a plan of the Congregation, but one against it: it wants to prevent what one of its own intends.

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › Bases and Means

- alt: The archive in the cellars of the Vatican lies in a part of the cellars that is on no plan, behind an inconspicuous door in a storeroom for old manuscripts. Only the Prefect and Brother Ludovico carry the key. Inside stand wooden crates with reports from three centuries, sorted by year and place, a long table with a lamp, a cabinet of index cards that refer to O.D.I.N. and are never complete, and the Mirror from Bamberg in its lead box. You get in with a favour for the Congregation or because Brother Ludovico believes you have earned it. ¶ The chapel of Santa Maria del Silenzio is a small chapel in a narrow lane in Trastevere. In the side chapel stands an old confessional in which the priests of the Congregation hear confessions. Behind the altar lies a room where the Twelve meet when a decision is due. The chapel is open like any other; the room behind the altar is not. ¶ Don Matteo Riva’s flat is on the third floor of an old house in Trastevere. On the desk is a laptop with an article, on the wall hangs a photo of his brother, and the telephone rings when the Congregation calls. He does not pick up.
- neu: The archive in the cellars of the Vatican lies in a part of the cellars that appears on no plan, behind an inconspicuous door in a storeroom for old manuscripts. Only the Prefect and Brother Ludovico carry the key. Inside are wooden crates with reports from three centuries, sorted by year and place, a long table with a lamp, a cabinet with index cards that refer to O.D.I.N. and are never complete, and the mirror from Bamberg in its lead box. One gets in with a favour for the Congregation or because Brother Ludovico believes one has earned it. ¶ The Chapel of Santa Maria del Silenzio is a small chapel in a narrow alley in Trastevere. In the side chapel stands an old confessional in which the priests of the Congregation hear confessions. Behind the altar lies a room in which the Twelve meet when a decision is pending. The chapel is open like any other; the room behind the altar is not. ¶ Don Matteo Riva’s flat is on the third floor of an old house in Trastevere. On the desk stands a laptop with an article, on the wall hangs a photo of his brother, and the telephone rings when the Congregation calls. He does not pick up.

- alt: People: Twelve members, priests and laypeople all over Europe. No fighters, no agents, but in almost every diocese someone who writes a report when a child sings in its sleep. ¶ Equipment and artefacts: The archive itself, the Mirror from Bamberg and a lead box for reports that are not left lying in the open. ¶ Connections: The Congregation knows the Lodge of the Silent Circle, because both came into being in 1872 from the same night. It knows the Hands from the confessional and the Investigation Branch from the archive.
- neu: People: Twelve members, priests and lay people across Europe. No fighters, no agents, but in almost every diocese someone who writes a report when a child sings in their sleep. ¶ Equipment and artefacts: The archive itself, the mirror from Bamberg and a lead box for reports that one does not leave lying open. ¶ Connections: The Congregation knows the Lodge of the Silent Circle, because both arose from the same night in 1872. It knows the Hands from the confessional and the Investigation Branch from the archive.

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › Key Figures

- alt: 68 years old, slight, white hair, glasses that he takes off and polishes when he is thinking. He has led the Congregation since 2009 and has reorganised the archive. He knows every report and gives only what one has earned. He found his predecessor’s unanswered letter of 1971 and has wondered ever since whether the Congregation’s silence is not also cowardice. He speaks slowly and weighs every word.
- neu: 68 years old, slim, white hair, glasses that he takes off and polishes when he thinks. He has led the Congregation since 2009 and reorganised the archive. He knows every report and gives only what one has earned. He found his predecessor’s unanswered letter from 1971 and has wondered ever since whether the Congregation’s silence is not also cowardice. He speaks slowly and weighs every word.

- alt: Behaviour Waits, examines, keeps silent. Talks only to someone who brings him proof that they deserve it
- neu: Behaviour Waits, tests, remains silent. Talks only to someone who brings him proof that they deserve it

- alt: 34 years old, dark eyes, a narrow face that has not seen sleep for weeks. He believes that the Church must not keep silent when children disappear. His secret: his little brother Paolo disappeared in 2019 from a care home near Lyon, nine years old, and Don Matteo believes that the Hands took him. He speaks quickly, with an urgency that borders on desperation. He is no fighter, but he is ready to risk everything.
- neu: 34 years old, dark eyes, a narrow face that has not seen sleep for weeks. He believes the Church must not remain silent when children disappear. He speaks quickly, with an urgency bordering on desperation. He is not a fighter, but he is ready to risk everything.

- alt: Weakness His conscience. He cannot keep silent when he believes that children are suffering ¶ Behaviour Pushes, talks, looks for allies. Flees when he believes that O.D.I.N. is coming for him
- neu: Weakness His conscience. He cannot remain silent when he believes children are suffering ¶ Behaviour Pushes, talks, seeks allies. Flees when he believes that O.D.I.N. is coming for him

- alt: 79 years old, stooped, a quiet, brittle voice. He has been hearing confessions for forty years and is one of the two priests who have listened to members of the Hands. He does not know O.D.I.N. as an organisation, only as a burden that people carry to him. Once he refused absolution to a man from the Hands because he did not believe his remorse, and to this day he does not know whether that was right. He recognises a Hand when he sees one. He never says so, but to an agent who trusts him he can give a hint without breaking the seal: Insight against Hard (3) to understand what he does not say.

- alt: 51 years old, broad, quiet, grey beard, hands that smell of paper. He enters every report that arrives and has never read one that was not meant for him, except for one: a report about his own village, which he put aside without reporting it. He protects the archive with his body if he has to.

He is not a key figure in this plan, but he can be at the scene at any time, ahead of the cell. Stats: Exorcist of the Congregation (Threat Atlas).

### 12 The Congregation of the Silent Word › Relationship to O.D.I.N.

- alt: Who at O.D.I.N. deals with them: The Antiquarians of the Investigation Branch, who are allowed into the archive in exchange for favours. The Registrar knows the Congregation, respects it and does not trust it.
- neu: Who at O.D.I.N. deals with them: The Antiquarians of the Investigation Branch, who may access the Archive in exchange for favours. The Registrar knows the Congregation, respects it and does not trust it.

- alt: What the Congregation knows about O.D.I.N.: That O.D.I.N. guards the circles and takes the children. That it is necessary and damned. It does not know the name of the Council or where the Holds are, and it does not ask.
- neu: What the Congregation knows about O.D.I.N.: That O.D.I.N. guards the circles and fetches the children. That it is necessary and damned. It does not know what the Council is called or where the Holds are, and it does not ask.

- alt: Absolution. Someone who listens without recording: a conversation with the Prefect in which an agent can say everything that may never go into a file. ¶ In return the Congregation wants nothing. Not yet. But the favour will be called in when it needs someone to open a door or protect a child.
- neu: Absolution. Someone who listens without taking notes: a conversation with the Prefect in which an agent can say everything that may never go into a file. ¶ The Congregation wants nothing for it. Not yet. But the favour will be called in when it needs someone to open a door or protect a child.

### 08 The Partners › The Liaison Persons

- alt: A woman of around fifty, grey coat, short dark hair, glasses with thin frames. She sits in cafés near railway stations, always facing the door, always with a newspaper she does not read. She drinks espresso, not café au lait. She pays in cash and leaves no tip. ¶ Marguerite Vasseur wants France to stay in the game. She took over the Veil Protocol from her predecessor, together with a notebook in which every favour France has done O.D.I.N. since 1961 is recorded. She keeps the book going. She does not believe in the supernatural, she believes in balances. O.D.I.N. is a factor one takes into account, not an ally one trusts. ¶ She speaks quietly, in short sentences, and she asks questions whose answers she already knows. She never says “O.D.I.N.”. She says “your people” or “your organisation”, as if she did not want to put the name in her mouth. When she grants a favour, it sounds like a statement: “That can be arranged.” When she asks for one, it sounds like a request that is not one: “There is one matter you could take care of for me.”
- neu: A woman around fifty, grey coat, short dark hair, glasses with thin frames. She sits in cafés near railway stations, always facing the door, always with a newspaper she does not read. She drinks espresso, not café au lait. She pays in cash and leaves no tip. ¶ Marguerite Vasseur wants France to remain in the game. She took over the Veil Protocol from her predecessor, together with a notebook in which every favour France has done O.D.I.N. since 1961 is recorded. She keeps the book going. She does not believe in the supernatural, she believes in balances. O.D.I.N. is a factor to be reckoned with, not an ally to be trusted. ¶ She speaks quietly, in short sentences, and she asks questions whose answers she already knows. She never says “O.D.I.N.”. She says “your people” or “your organisation”, as if she did not want to speak the name. When she grants a favour, it sounds like a statement: “That can be arranged.” When she asks for one, it sounds like a request that is not one: “I have a matter you could take care of for me.”

- alt: He talks as if chatting about the weather, even when he is explaining that he can make a body disappear from a mortuary. He likes to use the phrases “That might possibly be arranged” and “I fear that is beyond my means”. He makes no threats. He says sentences like: “I am sure you understand that my service would expect a certain consideration in this matter.”
- neu: He talks as if he were discussing the weather, even when he is explaining that he can make a body disappear from a morgue. He likes to use the phrases “That could possibly be arranged” and “I’m afraid that is beyond my capabilities.” He makes no threats. He says sentences like: “I’m sure you understand that my service would expect a certain consideration in this matter.”

- alt: A woman of around forty, blonde hair, weatherproof jacket, a rucksack instead of a handbag. She meets in libraries, museums, sometimes on a ferry. She drinks coffee from a thermos mug that she brings herself. She is the only liaison person who would shake the cell’s hand if she did not know that nobody does that. ¶ Ingrid Moen wants the restricted zones in Scandinavia to stay quiet. Her service has had a special relationship with O.D.I.N. since Nordlicht, and she has read her predecessors’ files from 1977, when a base in northern Norway was empty overnight and her service only counted the gaps in the reports. She knows that there are things one does not understand, and that O.D.I.N. understands them, or at least pretends to. She wants O.D.I.N. to stay strong in Scandinavia, because there are things there that nobody else guards. For that she is prepared to give more than other liaison persons, but she also asks for more: information about everything that happens in her area of responsibility. ¶ She says what she thinks, without beating about the bush. “I can do that. I cannot do that. I can do that, but then I want to know why.” She asks questions that other liaison persons would not ask, because she really wants to know what is going on. She says “O.D.I.N.” without hesitation, as if it were the name of a neighbour. She makes no jokes, but she smiles when someone is honest with her.

- alt: A man of around fifty, a dark suit one size too big, a briefcase that he holds with both hands. He meets in small restaurants, always near the harbour, always in the late afternoon. He eats nothing, he drinks a glass of water, and he keeps looking around, as if he expected someone to be watching him. ¶ Tomás Ferreira wants Portugal to stay out of the headlines. His service is small, and for him the Veil Protocol is a burden he inherited without knowing why. He has read the file on Diplomat, the man who died in Lisbon in 1994 as a gardener, or so his file says; the truth is in the Agent Building Block. He has understood that there are things one had better not ask about. He helps O.D.I.N. because he is afraid of what happens if he does not. But he only ever helps as much as necessary, and in return he asks that O.D.I.N. leave Portugal in peace. ¶ He speaks in a quiet, almost apologetic voice, as if constantly asking forgiveness for being there. He often says “I will see what can be done” and “I cannot promise anything”. He asks no questions, because he does not want to hear the answers. When he asks for a favour, it sounds like a plea for help, not like a demand.

His service is small, and the Veil Protocol is a burden he has inherited without knowing why. He has read the file on Diplomat, the man who died in Lisbon in 1994 as a gardener, so it says in his file; the truth is in the Agent Building Block. But he always helps only as much as necessary, and in return he demands that O.D.I.N. leave Portugal alone. ¶ He speaks in a quiet, almost apologetic voice, as if he were constantly asking forgiveness for being there. He often says “I’ll see what can be done” and “I can’t promise anything.” He asks no questions, because he does not want to hear the answers. When he asks for a favour, it sounds like a request for help, not like a demand.

### 08 The Partners › Hooks

- alt: Grey File “The False Passport”. An agent of the cell is detained at the border between France and Spain. His diplomatic passport is genuine, but the number is marked as stolen in a database. The handler calls Marguerite Vasseur, and she says: “That can be arranged. But there is one matter.” The matter is a man in Marseille who works for the French service and has not checked in for three days. The cell is to find him before the police do. ¶ Grey File “The Copy”. In London, a police file has turned up about an incident that should never have been put on record: a rift in a warehouse in Deptford that O.D.I.N. closed in 2019. Alistair Finch had the file closed, but someone has sent a copy to a journalist. The cell is to find out who, and retrieve the copy. Finch offers to have the story smothered through official channels: close the file, calm the editorial office, discredit the source. The cell must decide whether to allow that. ¶ Grey File “The Silent Border”. In northern Norway, a child has disappeared, from a foster family near Tromsø. The local police have closed the file, but Ingrid Moen has reopened it, because she knows what happens in this region. She asks the cell to find out whether someone in the region is taking children from foster families or whether something else has taken the child. She asks only one thing: that the cell afterwards reports to her what took the child.

The cell is to find him before the police do. ¶ Grey File “The Copy”. Finch offers to smother the story through official channels: close the file, calm the editorial office, discredit the source. The cell must decide whether to allow that. ¶ Grey File “The Silent Border”. In northern Norway, a child has disappeared, from a foster family near Tromsø. She asks the cell to find out whether someone in the region is taking children from foster families or whether something else took it. She demands only one thing: that the cell reports to her afterwards what took the child. She asks only one thing: that the cell afterwards reports to her what took the child.

### 08 The Partners › How the Protocol Works

- alt: When something has gone wrong, the cell meets the liaison person. That is the rule. An agent is in custody, a legend is burned, a car is parked in the wrong place outside a crime scene. Then the handler calls, and the next morning the liaison person sits in a café, with a newspaper and an envelope. They always pay. They make no jokes. They say what they can do, and what it costs.
- neu: When something has gone wrong, the cell meets the liaison person. That is the rule. An agent is in custody, a cover is blown, a car is parked badly in front of a crime scene. Then the handler calls, and the next morning the liaison sits in a café, with a newspaper and an envelope. They always pay. They make no jokes. They say what they can do, and what it costs.

### 08 The Partners › What the Partners Are

- alt: The British service, the French one and a dozen others are silent partners of the Veil Protocol. They never signed in 1961, because there are no signatures. They understood that there are things one does not bring before a committee, and that there is an organisation that takes care of them. In return, they keep the Veil tight from their side. ¶ The Partners provide what O.D.I.N. needs in the world of passports, files and borders: diplomatic passports that raise no questions. Police files that are closed before a journalist finds them. An aircraft that takes off at night from a military airfield without appearing in the logbook. A prisoner who is released from the cell in the morning because the right office has called. ¶ They do not ask questions. That is the core of the agreement. A partner service does not want to know why a man with a forged passport has to get to Helsinki or why a woman in Lyon needs a flat that is registered to no one. It wants to know that it is done, and that in return no one speaks its name. ¶ What a partner does not provide is just as important: no weapons, no operatives, no information about its own operations. A partner opens doors, it does not walk through them. Whoever asks a partner service for more gets a polite no and a note in a file that O.D.I.N. will never see.
- neu: The British service, the French one and a dozen others are silent partners of the Veil Protocol. They never signed in 1961, because there are no signatures. They understood that there are things one does not take to a committee, and that there is an organisation that takes care of them. In return, they keep the Veil tight on their side. ¶ The Partners deliver what O.D.I.N. needs in the world of passports, files and borders: diplomatic passports that raise no questions. Police files that are closed before a journalist finds them. An aircraft that takes off from a military airfield at night without appearing in the logbook. A prisoner who is released from the cell in the morning because the right office called. ¶ They do not ask questions. That is the core of the arrangement. A partner service does not want to know why a man with a forged passport has to go to Helsinki or why a woman in Lyon needs a flat registered to no one. It wants to know that it is done, and that in return no one names its name. ¶ What a Partner does not deliver is just as important: no weapons, no operational forces, no information about its own operations. A partner opens doors, it does not walk through them. Anyone who asks a partner service for more receives a polite no and a note in a file O.D.I.N. will never see.

### 08 The Partners › What the Partner Asks in Return for a Favour

- alt: 1 A piece of information. The partner wants to know what O.D.I.N. is doing in a particular region, or what lies behind an incident that has turned up in its files. The cell must answer a question, honestly and completely. ¶ 2 A silence. The partner has an operation running that O.D.I.N. could disturb. The cell is to make a report disappear, calm a witness or postpone an operation.
- neu: 1 A piece of information. The partner wants to know what O.D.I.N. is doing in a certain region, or what is behind an incident that has appeared in their files. The cell must answer one question, honestly and completely. ¶ 2 A silence. The partner has an operation running that could disrupt O.D.I.N. The cell is to make a report disappear, calm a witness or postpone an operation.

- alt: 4 An address. The partner wants to know where a person is who works or has worked for O.D.I.N. Not to harm them, but to watch them. The cell is to provide the address.
- neu: 4 An address. The partner wants to know where a person is who works or has worked for O.D.I.N. Not to harm them, but to observe them. The cell is to provide the address.

- alt: 6 A favour in the future. The partner asks for nothing concrete. It says: “You owe me something.” The game master notes the favour and calls it in later, when it is most inconvenient.
- neu: 6 A favour in the future. The partner asks for nothing concrete. They say: “You owe me something.” The game master notes the favour and redeems it later, when it is most inconvenient.

- alt: A favour is a currency that circulates between the cell and a partner service. For each partner, the game master notes whether it owes the cell something or the other way round. Calling in a noted favour succeeds without a test; if the cell asks for more, dice are rolled, two steps easier. A favour that the partner owes the cell expires after one campaign year. A favour that the cell owes never expires.

Calling in a noted favour succeeds without a test; if the cell asks for more, dice are rolled, two steps easier. A favour the partner owes the cell expires after one campaign year. A favour that the cell owes never expires.

### 08 The Partners › Overview

- alt: The Partners are not an organisation and not an alliance. They are a network of silent agreements between O.D.I.N. and the services that have supported the Veil Protocol since 1961 without ever having signed it. They provide what O.D.I.N. needs in the world of passports, files and borders, and they do not ask questions. In return, O.D.I.N. keeps the Veil tight from their side, and no one speaks their name.
- neu: The Partners are not an organisation and not an alliance. They are a network of silent agreements between O.D.I.N. and the services that have supported the Veil Protocol since 1961 without ever having signed it. They provide what O.D.I.N. needs in the world of passports, files and borders, and they do not ask questions. In return, O.D.I.N. keeps the Veil tight on their side, and no one names their name.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Expansion 5: Connections

- alt: A connection takes effect only once: when the triggering plan reaches the stage for the first time. If it falls back and rises again, nothing new happens. Chain reactions are allowed, but they take time. If a plan reaches a stage through a connection, it triggers its own connection only after the next mission. In this way a change spreads through the world like a wave, one mission per step, and each time the cell has the opportunity to stop it. ¶ Connections also take effect for plans the game master is not currently keeping an eye on. That is why the Situation Sheet lists all plans, not only the two or three the game master rolls for.

Thus a change spreads through the world like a wave, one mission per step, and the cell has the opportunity to stop it each time. ¶ Connections also take effect for plans the game master is not currently watching.
