The Singers hear the song from Anderlecht and find another staircase. ¶ “Keep the Silence” (Congregation of the Silent Word) ☠☠☠☠☠ “The Second Light” (The Enlightened) gains a skull. The article about vanished children feeds every conspiracy group in Europe, and “Illuminati Europa” gains new members. ¶ “Taking the Children” (The Open Ones) ☠☠☠☠○ “The Chimera” (Morrow Biologics) gains a skull. The Open Ones take a young woman with a dormant gift from the subject register of study MB-7 before she is enrolled, and the corporation accelerates its research in Lyon. ¶ “Taking the Children” (The Open Ones) ☠☠☠☠○ “Keep the Silence” (Congregation of the Silent Word) loses a skull. The Congregation sees that the Open Ones save children, and Don Matteo doubts whether his article is the right answer. ¶ “Taking the Children” (The Open Ones) ☠☠☠☠☠ “No More Nexus Crystals” (The Foundation in Basel) loses a skull. The Open Ones buy Nexus crystals for their choir themselves and take sources from the Foundation. ¶ “No More Nexus Crystals” (The Foundation in Basel) ☠☠☠○○ “The Great Stone” (The Evening) loses a skull. The Foundation steals a crystal from a warehouse of the Evening.

- alt: “No More Nexus Crystals” (The Foundation in Basel) ☠☠☠☠☠ “Taking the Children” (The Open Ones) gains a skull. The Hands no longer take children, and the Open Ones find the self-awakened undisturbed. ¶ “The Great Stone” (The Evening) ☠☠☠○○ “The Chimera” (Morrow Biologics) loses a skull. The Evening ties up all couriers and channels for the stone and no longer supplies Morrow with samples.
- neu: “No More Nexus Crystals” (The Foundation in Basel) ☠☠☠☠☠ “Taking the Children” (The Open Ones) gains a skull. The Hands no longer fetch children, and the Open Ones find the self-awakened undisturbed. ¶ “The Great Stone” (The Evening) ☠☠☠○○ “The Chimera” (Morrow Biologics) loses a skull. The Evening ties up all couriers and channels for the stone and no longer delivers samples to Morrow.

- alt: “The Great Stone” (The Evening) ☠☠☠☠☠ “The Page from 1871” (Lodge of the Silent Circle) gains a skull. In the excitement over the great stone, the Council leaves the Registry unwatched, and the Lodge gets closer to the page.
- neu: “The Great Stone” (The Evening) ☠☠☠☠☠ “The Page from 1871” (Lodge of the Silent Circle) gains a skull. In the excitement over the great stone, the Council leaves the Registry unobserved, and the Lodge gets closer to the page.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Chapter I: Asklepios and Its Heirs

- alt: Not every danger comes from outside. The two organisations of this chapter grew out of work that O.D.I.N. itself began or should never have allowed: out of a programme that was meant to heal people, and out of samples that came from rifts and wandered into other people’s laboratories. What connects them is the body. Here no one calls and no one sings, here things are changed, bred, carried. ¶ The Twelve of Asklepios are neither a service nor a cult, but an in-house legacy: nanites from the Asklepios programme that have been singing in time with the Opener since 2019, and twelve people who carry them inside them. They know more than they should know and have almost no means. Morrow Biologics is the opposite: a corporation with money, laboratories and lawyers that has no idea what it is working with. Morrow thinks cells from rifts are a discovery. The Twelve know that they are not. ¶ The game master uses the Twelve when the cell is to look inwards, into the clinic beneath the Institute and into the files of the Science Branch. Morrow suits missions in the ordinary world: studies, test subjects, press officers and a laboratory in Lyon in which something is growing. The two plans touch. Whoever follows the one sooner or later comes across the other.
- neu: Not every threat comes from outside. The two organisations in this chapter arose from work that O.D.I.N. itself began or that it should never have allowed: from a programme meant to heal people, and from samples taken from rifts that found their way into foreign laboratories. What connects them is the body. Here nothing is called and nothing is sung; here things are altered, bred, carried. ¶ The Twelve of Asklepios are not a service and not a cult, but a legacy within O.D.I.N.’s own walls: nanites from the Asklepios programme that have been singing in the Opener’s rhythm since 2019, and twelve people who carry them inside themselves. They know more than they should know, and have almost no resources. Morrow Biologics is the opposite: a corporation with money, laboratories and lawyers that has no idea what it is working with. Morrow thinks cells from rifts are a discovery. The Twelve know that they are not. ¶ The game master uses the Twelve when the cell should look inward, into the clinic beneath the Institute and into the files of the Science Branch. Morrow suits operations in the ordinary world: studies, test subjects, press spokespeople and a laboratory in Lyon where something is growing. The two plans touch. Whoever pursues one will sooner or later come upon the other.

- neu: The Twelve of Asklepios Asklepios Within, silent 1 3 “The new host” ☠○○○○

### The Other Services: Introduction › Chapter III: Foreign Services

- alt: O.D.I.N. is not the only service that knows there are rifts. The organisations of this chapter are state or semi-state bodies, they have budgets, identity cards and superiors, and they work where O.D.I.N. can only go covertly or not at all. What connects them is the question every service asks: what does the other side know, and how do you get at it? ¶ STILLWATER is the American rival that tolerates O.D.I.N. in North America and has now found a boy who hears the hum. Department 13 is the Russian enemy that uses up psions and has been searching for decades for a second site for alloy ore. The Seventh Bureau is a blank spot in China of which O.D.I.N. knows little more than a name and a report; the dossier offers three possible truths. The Partners , finally, are not opponents but the silent services that have helped carry the Veil Protocol since 1961 and name a price for every favour. ¶ The game master uses foreign services when a campaign is to grow larger than Europe or when the cell is to feel that O.D.I.N. is being watched. Foreign services rarely kill first. They watch, recruit, buy and wait. Real services remain a backdrop here: those who act are always the invented bodies that hide within them.
- neu: O.D.I.N. is not the only service that knows rifts exist. The organisations in this chapter are state or semi-state, they have budgets, identity cards and superiors, and they work where O.D.I.N. can only operate covertly or not at all. What unites them is the question every service asks: What does the other side know, and how do you get it? ¶ STILLWATER is the American rival that tolerates O.D.I.N. in North America and has now found a boy who hears humming. Department 13 is the Russian enemy that uses up Psions and has been searching for a second site of alloy ore for decades. The Seventh Bureau is a blank spot in China of which O.D.I.N. knows little more than a name and a report; the dossier offers three possible truths. The Partners , finally, are not opponents but the silent services that have supported the Veil Protocol since 1961 and name a price for every favour. ¶ The game master uses foreign services when a campaign is to become larger than Europe or when the cell is to feel that O.D.I.N. is being watched. Foreign services rarely kill first. They watch, recruit, buy and wait. Real services remain backdrop: those who act are always the invented offices hiding within them.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Expansion 1: Strength

- alt: Not every organisation advances equally fast. A corporation with laboratories in three countries has other means than an old man in a monastery. With this expansion, the roll after the mission depends on the strength of the organisation.
- neu: Not every organisation advances at the same speed. A corporation with laboratories in three countries has different resources than an old man in a monastery. With this expansion, the roll after the mission depends on the organisation’s strength.

- alt: Strong organisations advance faster, and the cell has to disrupt them more often to stop them. Weak ones need luck or help, and they often get help from other plans (Expansion 5). An organisation with Strength 0 has no plan.
- neu: Strong organisations advance faster, and the cell must disrupt them more often to stop them. Weak ones need luck or help, and they often get help from other plans (Expansion 5). An organisation with Strength 0 has no plan.

### The Other Services: Introduction › A Year in the World

- alt: An example over six missions. The game master has decided on three organisations that are to be important in her campaign: STILLWATER ( ☠☠○○○ ), the Open Ones ( ☠☠☠○○ ) and the Enlightened ( ☠☠☠○○ ). The other plans stand at their starting values on the Situation Sheet. ¶ 1. The cell plays File 8 “The Second Light” and ends the ritual in Anderlecht. The Enlightened lose a skull and stand at ☠☠○○○ . With Knowledge 2 they do not notice who it was; there is no countermove. After the mission the game master rolls: STILLWATER has Strength 4 and rises on a 5 to ☠☠☠○○ . The connection triggers: the Congregation of the Silent Word learns of a second Seeker from a confession and rises to ☠☠☠☠○ , although no one was keeping an eye on it. A Red File emerges. The Open Ones roll a 3; with Strength 2 they need a 6. ¶ 2. The Red File of the Congregation lands with the cell: the Prefect asks for help. The cell finds out what became of Don Matteo’s brother, and Don Matteo gives up the article. The key figure is taken out, the plan falls to ☠☠○○○ and does not roll next time. The Congregation has Knowledge 4: the countermove is a 5, a bait. The Prefect answers one question for the cell, and the cell owes him a favour. Rolls: STILLWATER a 2, the Open Ones a 5. Nothing rises. The game master places the trail of STILLWATER in the next mission: a psion of the cell hears a humming at night that does not come from O.D.I.N. ¶ 3. The cell follows the humming and finds a dead drop in Montréal with a message about a boy from Fairbanks. STILLWATER loses a skull and stands at ☠☠○○○ ; with Knowledge 3 the programme answers with a countermove from its dossier. Rolls: the Open Ones roll a 6 and stand at ☠☠☠☠○ . A Red File emerges, and two connections trigger: Morrow Biologics rises to ☠☠☠☠○ , the Congregation falls to ☠○○○○ . Morrow itself has a connection at ☠☠☠☠○ , but because the corporation rose through a connection, it waits until after the next mission. Now two Red Files are on the table. The game master gives the cell the more urgent one and leaves the other lying for one mission. ¶ 4. The cell takes the Red File of the Open Ones and reaches two of three children on the slip of paper from the Zurich safe deposit box before the couriers. The Open Ones fall to ☠☠☠○○ . With Knowledge 5 they know exactly who it was: the countermove is a 4, the voice in the sleep. A psion of the cell rolls his Resonance roll at the start of the next mission. Rolls: STILLWATER rolls a 4 and is back at ☠☠☠○○ ; the connection to the Congregation does not take effect again. The Open Ones roll a 1. Now Morrow’s waiting connection takes effect: the Evening gets money and channels and rises to ☠☠☠○○ . Its own connections wait. ¶ 5. The Red File left lying from Morrow Biologics: animal carcasses are moving in the sewers of Lyon. The cell exposes Dr. Camille Favre with the files on the dead researchers, and the corporation dismisses her. The key figure is taken out, Morrow falls to ☠☠○○○ . With Knowledge 1 the corporation has no idea who is behind it and sends Grey Meridian mercenaries to the wrong place. Rolls: STILLWATER rolls a 6 and stands at ☠☠☠☠○ ; a Red File emerges. The Open Ones roll a 2. Then the connections of the Evening take effect: Morrow gets no more samples and falls to ☠○○○○ , and Department 13 obtains information about O.D.I.N.’s alloy stocks through the Evening and rises to ☠☠☠☠○ . Another Red File. ¶ 6. The Red File of STILLWATER leads into the maintenance tunnels in Maryland. The cell cannot get to Dr. Webb, but it destroys the documents for the procedure. The plan falls to ☠☠☠○○ . Rolls: the Open Ones roll a 6 and are back at ☠☠☠☠○ ; their connections have already taken effect. Then the connection of Department 13 takes effect: the Americans fear coming away empty-handed on alloy ore, and STILLWATER rises again to ☠☠☠☠○ . ¶ At the end of the year no plan has reached the fifth skull. But the Situation Sheet shows a world that is more restless than at the beginning: the Open Ones, STILLWATER and Department 13 stand at ☠☠☠☠○ , three Red Files lie on the table, and the cell owes the Congregation a favour. The game master planned none of these developments. They arose from rolls, connections and the decisions of the cell, and the second year begins with Kantor calling a psion of the cell by name at night.

- alt: Plans that no one pursues may rest. A plan that stands at ☠○○○○ for two years is not negligence, but a world with many open fronts. ¶ The players should never have the feeling that the dice devalue their work. A good mission against a plan always takes at least one skull, even if the roll afterwards brings one back.

### The Other Services: Introduction › The Other Services

- alt: O.D.I.N. is not alone. It is the oldest organisation that knows what lies behind the Veil, and probably the most powerful. But it is not the only one searching, and not always the cleverest. A corporation in Leiden breeds cells from rifts. A programme in American services has found a boy who hears the hum. A small lodge in Uppsala knows more about the night of 1871 than the Registry. And in a monastery in the Carpathians, an old man in a wheelchair is waiting to sing once more. ¶ The Game Master’s Book sums up this world in a table, “Status 2026”, and in short paragraphs on each organisation. This volume builds full dossiers from it: sixteen dossiers in five chapters, with history, key figures, bases, means and a plan that grows level by level if no one stops it. The sixth chapter shows how the plans interlock and how the game master runs them across a whole campaign.
- neu: O.D.I.N. is not alone. It is the oldest organisation that knows what lies behind the Veil, and probably the most powerful. But it is not the only one that searches, and not always the wisest. A corporation in Leiden grows cells from rifts. A programme in American services has found a boy who hears humming. A small lodge in Uppsala knows more about the night of 1871 than the Registry. And in a monastery in the Carpathians, an old man in a wheelchair waits to sing once more. ¶ The Game Master’s Book summarises this world in a table, “Status 2026”, and in short paragraphs about each organisation. This volume builds complete dossiers from it: sixteen dossiers in five chapters, with history, leaders, bases, resources, and a plan that grows stage by stage if no one stops it. The sixth chapter shows how the plans interlock and how the game master runs them across an entire campaign.

### The Other Services: Introduction › How to Use This Volume

- alt: No one needs to know the whole volume to use it. The game master chooses two or three organisations that are to matter in their campaign, reads their dossiers and sets up the Situation Sheet (Chapter VI). The other dossiers stay on the shelf until a trail leads to them. That happens more often than you would think: the plans are connected, and whoever follows the Foundation eventually ends up at the Evening. ¶ The plans generate files. At the second skull, the cell receives a Grey File on this plan, at the fourth a Red one, at the fifth the Council takes over with a Black one. The hooks in each dossier are starting points, not finished missions. They name a place, an occasion and a catch; the rest the game master builds the way the Game Master’s Book describes. ¶ The scenarios in the volume “Grey Files” are tied to several plans of this volume: File 1 “Foster Child” to the Open Ones, File 2 “Find Site” to the Open Ones and the Evening, File 4 “Kessler” and File 7 “The Display Case” to the Foundation in Basel, File 5 “Second Circle” to Morrow Biologics, File 6 “The Tutor” to the Lodge of the Silent Circle and File 8 “The Second Light” to the Enlightened. Where a file moves a plan, this is stated in the box From the Grey Files at the end of the dossier.

The game master chooses two or three organisations that should be important in their campaign, reads their dossiers, and creates the situation sheet (Chapter VI). The remaining dossiers stay on the shelf until a sign leads to them. That happens more often than you would think: the plans are connected, and whoever follows the Foundation eventually ends up at the Evening. ¶ The plans generate files. At the second skull, the cell receives a Grey File for this plan; at the fourth, a Red File; at the fifth, the Council takes over with a Black File. The hooks in each dossier are starting points, not finished operations. They name a place, an occasion, and a catch; the rest is built by the game master as described in the Game Master’s Book. ¶ The scenarios in the volume “Grey Files” depend on several plans from this volume: File 1 “Foster Child” on the Open Ones, File 2 “Find Site” on the Open Ones and the Evening, File 4 “Kessler” and File 7 “The Display Case” on the Foundation in Basel, File 5 “Second Circle” on Morrow Biologics, File 6 “The Tutor” on the Lodge of the Silent Circle, and File 8 “The Second Light” on the Enlightened. Where a file moves a plan, this is noted in the box From the Grey Files at the end of the dossier.

### The Other Services: Introduction › The Situation Sheet

- alt: The game master keeps a sheet with all plans. After each mission she enters what has changed: the skulls, whether the key figure is still there and which trail she last laid. The sheet is the memory of the world. It shows at a glance which plan is becoming dangerous, and it prevents a connection from being forgotten.
- neu: The game master keeps a sheet with all plans. After each mission she enters what has changed: the skulls, whether the key figure is still there and which trail she last laid. The sheet is the memory of the world. It shows at a glance which plan is currently becoming dangerous, and it prevents a connection from being forgotten.

- alt: Congregation of the Silent Word “Keep the Silence” ☠☠☠○○ Don Matteo Riva
- neu: Congregation of the Silent Word “Preserve the silence” ☠☠☠○○ Don Matteo Riva

- alt: The Status column shows the starting value for the year 2026. Whoever begins with a different campaign adjusts the starting values, for instance if files from the “Grey Files” volume have already been played.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Chapter V: Renegades and Collectors

- alt: The Open Ones are psions without helmets under Kantor, the man who caused the Cascade in 1971. They fetch children out of foster families and know almost everything about the Choir. The Foundation in Basel buys Nexus crystals so that the Council does not get them, and Elster sits on its foundation board. The Evening is a travelling auction that sells everything that comes from rifts, and it has promised a bidder a stone as big as a person. Grey Meridian has no plan of its own. The firm supplies the mercenaries for the plans of the others, and sometimes it is the only opponent you can talk to. ¶ The game master uses this chapter when the cell is to ask itself which side it is on. The Open Ones and the Foundation are right on important points, and both know things about O.D.I.N. that the Council would rather forget. The Evening and Grey Meridian are simpler: they want money. But whoever buys from them or pays them changes the plans of everyone else.

The Evening is a travelling auction that sells everything that comes out of rifts, and has promised one bidder a stone as large as a person. Grey Meridian has no plan of its own. The company supplies the mercenaries for the plans of others, and sometimes it is the only opponent one can talk to. ¶ The game master uses this chapter when the cell should ask itself which side it is on. The Open Ones and the Foundation are right on important points, and both know things about O.D.I.N. that the Council would rather forget. The Evening and Grey Meridian are simpler: they want money. But whoever buys from them or pays them changes the plans of everyone else.

### The Other Services: Introduction › How a Dossier Is Structured

- alt: History. The most important years, and at the end a paragraph with what only the game master knows. ¶ What They Want. The organisation’s goal and why it believes itself to be in the right. ¶ Key Figures. The most important characters with appearance, secret and stats. Adversaries who already have stats in the Game Master’s Book or the Core Rulebook are only named, with a reference.

The organisation’s goal and why they believe they are in the right. ¶ Key Figures. The most important characters with appearance, secret and stats. Opponents who already have stats in the Game Master’s Book or the Core Rulebook are only named, with a reference.

- alt: Relationship to O.D.I.N. Who at O.D.I.N. deals with them, what both sides know about each other, and, in the box The Bait , what the organisation can use to lure an agent. ¶ The Plan. Six levels from ○○○○○ to ☠☠☠☠☠ , each with a trail by which the cell notices that something is moving. Plus the key figure.

Six stages from ○○○○○ to ☠☠☠☠☠ , each with a sign by which the cell notices that something is moving. Plus the key figure.

- alt: Connections. Which other plans rise or fall when this plan reaches a certain level.
- neu: Connections. Which other plans rise or fall when this plan reaches a certain stage.

- alt: Small dossiers are shorter and have parts of their own: the small cults, the Seventh Bureau with its three possible truths, the Partners with their favours, the Freemasons and Illuminati as a red herring, and Grey Meridian, the firm without a plan of its own.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Chapter VI: Connected Plans

- alt: The world does not wait for the cell. Eleven organisations in this volume have plans, and every plan has skulls that rise when no one is looking. The Game Master’s Book explains how a single plan grows and falls. This chapter expands the rule for the dossiers in this volume: it shows how the strength and knowledge of an organisation change the plan, what a key figure is, how the players learn about a plan from the world and how the plans interlock. At the end there is a Situation Sheet, with which the game master runs all plans at the same time without losing track, and a year as an example. ¶ The expansions do not replace the basic rule. Whoever uses only the basic rule can still play every dossier. Whoever uses the expansions gets a world in which the organisations are differently fast, differently clever and entangled with one another.

The Game Master’s Book explains how a single plan rises and falls. This chapter expands the rule for the dossiers in this volume: it shows how an organisation’s strength and knowledge change the plan, what a key figure is, how the players learn about a plan from the world, and how the plans interlock. At the end there is a situation sheet with which the game master runs all plans simultaneously without losing track, and a year as an example. ¶ The expansions do not replace the basic rule. Anyone using only the basic rule can still play every dossier. Anyone using the expansions gets a world in which the organisations are different in speed, different in cleverness, and interwoven with one another.

- alt: At the fifth, what is stated in the dossier happens. The Council takes over with a Black File, and the organisation gets a new plan.
- neu: At the fifth, what the dossier says happens. The Council takes over with a Black File, and the organisation gets a new plan.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Real Organisations

- alt: The world of O.D.I.N. is our world, with real intelligence services, churches, lodges and companies. They remain a backdrop. No real service, no real church and no real company is a perpetrator in this volume. STILLWATER hides in an invented front agency, Department 13 in a department that appears in no organisation chart, the Congregation of the Silent Word in a cellar that no plan shows. The Freemasons are not the conspiracy but the place where one can hide, and the dossier treats them with the respect that a long tradition deserves. The perpetrators are always the invented organisations.

STILLWATER hides in a fictional cover agency, Department 13 in a department that appears on no organisational chart, the Congregation of the Silent Word in a cellar that no map shows. The Freemasons are not the conspiracy but the place where one can hide, and the dossier treats them with the respect that a long tradition deserves. The perpetrators are always the fictional organisations.

- alt: Several dossiers touch on children in danger, experiments on humans and loss. Before the campaign, the game master agrees what is off-limits at the table and what is only hinted at (Game Master’s Book, chapter “Safety at the Table”). No plan in this volume requires the cell to harm a child, and no child is ever a key figure.

No plan in this volume requires the cell to harm a child, and no child is ever a key figure.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Expansion 2: Knowledge and Countermoves

- alt: Whoever knows how O.D.I.N. works also knows who has harmed them. If the cell takes one or two skulls from a plan and the organisation has Knowledge 3 or more, it answers with a countermove . The game master rolls a D6 on the table in the dossier: 1 to 2, 3 to 4, 5 to 6. ¶ A countermove hits the cell, not the plan. It changes no skulls. It is a bait offered to an agent, a threat against an anchor, a witness who disappears, a contact who suddenly falls silent. The game master places the countermove in the next mission, ideally where the cell does not expect it. ¶ Organisations with Knowledge 1 or 2 do not notice who is harming them. Instead of the table they have a section “How They React”, and their answer is more general: they move a laboratory, postpone an auction, send mercenaries to where no one is. Knowledge 5, as with the Open Ones, means: they almost always know which cell it was, and often also which agent.

The game master rolls a D6 on the table in the dossier: 1 to 2, 3 to 4, 5 to 6. ¶ A countermove hits the cell, not the plan. It changes no skulls. It is a lure offered to an agent, a threat against an anchor, a witness who disappears, a contact who suddenly goes silent. The game master places the countermove in the next mission, preferably where the cell does not expect it. ¶ Organisations with knowledge 1 or 2 do not notice who harms them. Instead of the table, they have a section “How they react”, and their response is more general: they move a laboratory, postpone an auction, send mercenaries to where no one is. Knowledge 5, as with the Open Ones, means: they almost always know which cell it was, and often also which agent.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Chapter IV: The Brotherhoods

- alt: Brotherhoods preserve. They preserve rituals, names, files and silence, some for centuries, some only for a few years. The four dossiers of this chapter describe communities that define themselves through oaths and degrees and that have more in common with O.D.I.N. than either side would like. What sets them apart is how much they really know. ¶ The Freemasons know nothing. They are a worldwide federation with a long tradition and, for O.D.I.N., a backdrop, and the Illuminati are a legend that the Print Shop has cultivated for decades to swallow real trails. Both are in the same dossier because the cell usually meets them as a red herring. The Lodge of the Silent Circle in Uppsala knows more about the night of 1871 than O.D.I.N. and wants back a page that lies in Stockholm. The Enlightened are a legend of the Print Shop that has gained real members and whose rituals have sometimes worked since 2011. The Congregation of the Silent Word in the Vatican guards reports from three centuries, and this year one of its own wants to talk. ¶ The game master uses brotherhoods when the cell is to search in archives, church registers and lodge houses. They are rarely violent and often willing to negotiate, but they forget nothing. Respect towards real communities is mandatory here: Freemasonry is not the conspiracy, but the place where one can hide.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Expansion 4: The Trail

- alt: Every stage of a plan has a trail : a rumour in the canteen, a newspaper report, a dead drop, a phone call, a humming, a measurement. The trails are in the right-hand column of every plan table. The game master places the trail of the current skull in the next mission, even if the mission is about something entirely different. ¶ Rule of thumb: every mission contains a trail to a plan that is currently rising. The players do not learn from the rulebook that something is moving, but from the world. Whoever notices the trail can decide whether to follow it. Whoever overlooks it learns at the next skull what they overlooked. A trail is never mandatory, and it is never so hidden that only the game master finds it.
- neu: Every stage of a plan has a sign : a rumour in the canteen, a newspaper report, a dead drop, a call, a hum, a measurement. The trails are in the right-hand column of every plan table. The game master places the sign of the current skull in the next mission, even if the mission is about something completely different. ¶ Rule of thumb: every mission contains a sign of a plan that is currently rising. The players do not learn from the rulebook that something is moving, but from the world. Whoever notices the sign can decide whether to follow it. Whoever overlooks it learns at the next skull what they overlooked. A sign is never mandatory, and it is never so hidden that only the game master finds it.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Chapter II: The Cults

- alt: Cults are the everyday business of investigative work. Most are small, confused and harmless, says the Core Rulebook, and that is true. The cults in this chapter are not. Each of them holds something in its hands that is real: a song, a crystal, a dead man who speaks in dreams, a staircase that leads deeper than a mountain can be. What connects them is faith, and what makes them dangerous is that their faith sometimes receives an answer. ¶ The Singers are the great cult of this volume, without a leader and without a book, grown up around the staircase at the Brocken. You do not smash them, you stop them, level by level. The small cults are five examples of what can arise in any small town in Europe: from the forest crystal to the community that is waiting for a landing. They are described briefly, each with a small plan of its own, and can be scattered into any campaign. ¶ The game master plays cults not as conspirators but as people who have found something and cannot let go of it any more. A cult is almost never the end of a trail but its beginning: whoever wants to know where the crystal a cult worships comes from finds a rift, a collector or a legend of the Print Shop.

- neu: The Circle of the Returned Observed 2 3 “The open door” ☠○○○○

### The Other Services: Introduction › Expansion 6: After the Fifth Skull

- alt: At the fifth skull, what is stated in the dossier happens, and the Council takes over with a Black File. The cell can play a role in this file, but it can no longer undo what has happened. The world has changed.
- neu: At the fifth skull, what is written in the dossier happens, and the Council takes over with a Black File. The cell can play a role in this file, but they can no longer undo what has happened. The world has changed.

- alt: After that, the organisation gets its next plan. It is at the end of every dossier, with its starting value. The old key figure can be the new one, but does not have to be.
- neu: After that, the organisation gets its next plan. It is at the end of each dossier, with its starting value. The old key figure can be the new one, but does not have to be.

### The Other Services: Introduction › Expansion 3: The Key Figure

- alt: Every plan hangs on one person. Without Dr. Camille Favre there is no Chimera, without Kantor no choir, without the bidder no great stone. This person is the key figure , and their name is in the short profile of every dossier. ¶ If the cell takes out the key figure, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. Taking out means: arresting, turning, persuading, exposing or weakening them so much that they can no longer lead the plan. Taking out does not mean killing. Most key figures are people who can be persuaded or exposed, and the Game Master’s Book rewards solutions without combat. Many dossiers say explicitly what happens if the cell chooses violence, and it is rarely good for the cell.
- neu: Every plan hangs on one person. Without Dr. Camille Favre there is no Chimera, without Kantor no choir, without the bidder no great stone. This person is the key figure , and their name is in the Profile of every dossier. ¶ If the cell neutralises the key figure, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. Neutralising means: arresting, turning, convincing, exposing, or weakening them so that they can no longer lead the plan. Taking out does not mean killing. Most key figures are people who can be convinced or exposed, and the Game Master’s Book rewards solutions without combat. Many dossiers explicitly state what happens if the cell chooses violence, and it is rarely good for the cell.

### 13 The Open Ones › Key Figures

- alt: Eighty-seven years old, thin, in a wheelchair, white hair down to his shoulders, eyes that look at no one for long because they are hearing something else. He was Ilse Marek’s successor and the man who caused the Cascade. He speaks quietly, kindly, in long sentences, as if he had time that others no longer have. He explains, he offers, he asks for the name a psion bore before the Opening. His secret: he believes that Märta is alive, on the other side, and that she is the voice he has heard every night since 1971. He wants to answer her before he dies.
- neu: Eighty-seven years old, thin, in a wheelchair, white hair down to his shoulders, eyes that do not look at anyone for long because they hear something else. He was Ilse Marek’s successor and the man who caused the Cascade. He speaks quietly, kindly, in long sentences, as if he had time that others no longer have. He explains, he offers, he asks for the name a psion bore before the Opening. His secret: He believes that Märta lives, on the other side, and that she is the voice he has heard every night since 1971. He wants to answer her before he dies.

- alt: Special Resonance 5. Hears every psion without a helmet within one kilometre and every child whose gift breaks through within a hundred kilometres. Cannot be located by powers that require a helmet
- neu: Special Resonance 5. Hears every Psion without a helmet within one kilometre and every child whose gift breaks through within one hundred kilometres. Cannot be located by powers that require a helmet

- alt: Thirty-four, slim, short hair, calm hands, a grey coat. Was himself an opened child and took off his helmet when he learned what the Opening is. He is Kantor’s man for the north: Hamburg, Bremen, Copenhagen. He speaks first and fights last, and he does not fight to kill but to gain time. His secret: he does not know whether his foster parents loved him, and he asks every child he takes whether it loves its own. Stats: Open One (Elite), Game Master’s Book, Psion Building Block; see also Grey Files, File 1.
- neu: Thirty-four, thin, short hair, calm hands, a grey coat. Was himself an opened child and took off his helmet when he learned what the Opening is. He is Kantor’s man for the north: Hamburg, Bremen, Copenhagen. He speaks first and fights last, and he does not fight to kill but to gain time. His secret: He does not know whether his foster parents loved him, and he asks every child he fetches whether they love theirs. Stats: Open One (Elite), Game Master’s Book, Psion Building Block; see also Grey Files, File 1.

- alt: Twenty-nine, dark hair, a small scar behind her left ear, and she does not know where it comes from. Kantor took her from a foster family in Bucharest in 2006. She runs the monastery when Kantor sleeps, and these days that is often. She speaks quickly and quietly, as if someone could be listening. Her secret: she remembers her Opening, a white room, chalk on the floor and a voice that said her name. That is impossible, because the Sleep takes this memory from every child. She has told no one, not even Kantor, because she fears what he would make of it.

She has told no one, not even Kantor, because she fears what he would make of it.

- alt: Weakness Her memory. A psion who tells her that he remembers too, true or not, gets her talking: Insight against Hard (3) to notice that she is listening ¶ Behaviour Protects the children first and Kantor second. Negotiates if the cell leaves her time, and flees with the children if not
- neu: Weakness Her memory. A Psion who tells her that they also remember, whether true or not, makes her talk: Insight against Hard (3) to notice that she is listening ¶ Behaviour Protects the children first and Kantor second. Negotiates if the cell gives her time, and flees with the children if not

- alt: Nineteen, tall, quiet, a cap pulled low over his forehead. The Open Ones took him at nine from a foster family near Iași, before the Hands came. He knows every route between the Carpathians and the Rhine, every mill, every locker. His gift never awoke, and he is glad of it. He carries a compact pistol he has never used and papers from Elster’s workshop. His secret: once, out of curiosity, he put on a helmet he had taken from a child, and since then he hears a humming at night that he takes for his own. Stats: Courier of the Open Ones (Professional), Game Master’s Book, Agent Building Block.
- neu: Nineteen, tall, quiet, a cap pulled low over his forehead. The Open Ones fetched him at nine from a foster family near Iași, before the Hands came. He knows every route between the Carpathians and the Rhine, every mill, every locker. His gift never awakened, and he is glad of it. He carries a compact pistol he has never used and papers from Elster’s workshop. His secret: He once put on a helmet he had taken from a child, out of curiosity, and since then he hears a hum at night that he believes is his own. Stats: Courier of the Open Ones (Professional), Game Master’s Book, Agent Building Block.

### 13 The Open Ones › History and Goals

- alt: In 1970 an ambitious telepath with the codename Kantor takes over the Psionic Core from Dr. Ilse Marek. He believes that the Choir can do more than the rules allow, and that the helmets take more from the psions than they protect. ¶ In October 1971 Kantor seats forty psions without helmets in a circle in a hall in Wales. Three die of cerebral haemorrhages, seven lose their abilities for ever. Kantor lies in a coma for two weeks and, on waking, says the sentence that has stood in every file since. The Council introduces the Rule of the Twelve and removes Kantor from his post.
- neu: In 1970 an ambitious telepath with the codename Kantor takes over the Psionic Core from Dr. Ilse Marek. He believes that the Choir can do more than the rules allow, and that the helmets take more from Psions than they protect. ¶ In October 1971 Kantor places forty Psions without helmets in a circle in a hall in Wales. Three die of cerebral haemorrhages, seven lose their abilities for ever. Kantor lies in a coma for two weeks and, after waking, says the sentence that has been in every file since. The Council introduces the Rule of the Twelve and removes Kantor from his post.

- alt: In 1976 Echo operatives under Paul Mercier arrest seven Open Ones in the monastery of Sfântul Ioan, among them a fifteen-year-old. Kantor is not there that night. Two years later the Open Ones return to the same place because they have no better one. In the Choir no one has forgotten the operation, and among the Open Ones even less so. ¶ In 1989 Elster brings the register of the Nursery to the Carpathians: the real names of all Hands since 1952, of their escorts from the Tactical Branch and of the foster families. She does not sell it, she gives it to Kantor. Within a year the Open Ones find sixty names on the list. In a dozen foster families a child is missing one morning. The Council has it spread that a female agent sold real names to a foreign buyer, and most agents believe it to this day. ¶ From 1990 to 2011 the Open Ones work quietly. They take children from foster families they find through the list and bring them to the Carpathians by way of mills, flats and station lockers. Some of these children become couriers, some Open Ones, most just adults with a different name and a humming that never quite goes away. ¶ In 2011 the Seeker Muezzin dies, and the new Seeker hears more children than all before her. The number of psions triples, the Hands take more children than ever before, and the 1989 list grows old. Kantor needs a new way to find the children, and he finds it: he hears them himself. ¶ By 2026 the Open Ones have taken over two hundred children from foster families since 1989. Kantor is eighty-seven and sits in a wheelchair. He says he wants to sing once more before he dies, with voices that are free. In Hamburg-Neugraben Jonas Vogt is watching a foster family whose child hums at night. ¶ What only the game master knows: Kantor did not choose the site of the Cascade himself. The numbers station showed it to him, a hall above the third tunnel of the Chamber, and in the weeks before he wrote letters to a house without a number in Stockholm (Game Master’s Book, The Whole Truth). He believes it was a woman on the radio, Agnes, and that she spoke for Märta. Who really stood behind the numbers, he does not know. On the night of the Cascade Märta died, the first Seeker. Kantor believes that she did not die but crossed over, and that she has been waiting ever since. He believes he understands the Thirteenth. He has never spoken with her. He has only listened, for fifty-five years, and what he has heard he takes for a choir that has had half its voices taken from it.

Two years later the Open Ones return to the same place because they have no better one. In the Choir no one has forgotten the operation, and among the Open Ones even less so. ¶ 1989 Elster brings the register of the Nursery to the Carpathians: the real names of all Hands since 1952, their escorts from the Tactical Branch, and the foster families. They fetch children from foster families they find via the list and bring them to the Carpathians via mills, flats, and station lockers. Some of these children become couriers, some Open Ones, most just adults with a different name and a hum that never quite goes away. ¶ In 2011 the Seeker Muezzin dies, and the new Seeker hears more children than all before her. The number of Psions triples, the Hands fetch more children than ever before, and the 1989 list grows old. Kantor needs a new way to find the children, and he finds it: he hears them himself. ¶ 2026 the Open Ones have fetched over two hundred children from foster families since 1989. Kantor is eighty-seven and sits in a wheelchair. He says he wants to sing once more before he dies, with voices that are free. Jonas Vogt observes a foster family in Hamburg-Neugraben whose child hums at night. ¶ What only the game master knows: Kantor did not choose the site of the Cascade himself. The numbers station showed it to him, a hall above the third tunnel of the Chamber, and in the weeks before, he wrote letters to a house without a number in Stockholm (Game Master’s Book, The Truth). He believes it was a woman on the radio, Agnes, and that she spoke for Märta. Who really stood behind the numbers, he does not know. On the night of the Cascade, Märta, the first Seeker, died. Kantor believes that she did not die but went over, and that she has been waiting ever since. He believes he understands the Thirteenth. He has never spoken with her. He has only listened, for fifty-five years, and what he has heard he takes for a choir from which half its voices have been taken.

- alt: The Open Ones want to end the Opening by taking the children from O.D.I.N. Every child they take is a child who does not sit in a white room while the Thaumaturges read a circle. They know that the Opening costs every third child. They know that the foster families are legends. They know that the children who go at an opening do not die but go, and they believe they know where. ¶ Kantor wants more than that. He wants to bring the children he has taken and the psions who have followed him together in a choir of their own, without helmets, without the Opening, without Observers. He believes that such a choir does not call the Thirteenth but answers her, and that an answer is something other than a rift. He is right about one thing: the Opening is a crime. Whether he is right about the second, no one knows, and the Open Ones are ready to find out. ¶ They believe they are in the right because they are the only ones who ask the children. No child the Open Ones take goes against its will. That, at least, is what they say. Most of the children are eight, nine, ten years old, and they want the humming to stop.

No child the Open Ones fetch goes against their will. That, at least, is what they say. Most of the children are eight, nine, ten years old, and they want the hum to stop.

### 13 The Open Ones › Overview

- alt: The Open Ones are psions who have taken off their helmets and will never put them on again. They consider the Opening a crime, the foster families prisons with curtains, and O.D.I.N. an organisation that sacrifices children and calls it protection. They are few, they are old or very young, and they know more about the Choir, the Hands and the Nursery than most agents who work there. For the game master they are the adversary who is right about one thing. Perhaps about two.
- neu: The Open Ones are psions who have taken off their helmets and will never put them on again. They consider the Opening a crime, foster families prisons with curtains, and O.D.I.N. an organisation that sacrifices children and calls it protection. They are few, they are old or very young, and they know more about the Choir, the Hands and the Nursery than most agents who work there. For the game master, they are the opponent who is right about one thing. Perhaps about two.

### 13 The Open Ones › Relationship to O.D.I.N.

- alt: What O.D.I.N. knows: That Kantor is alive and leads the Open Ones. That they take children from foster families. Where their monastery lies, since 1976. The Council tolerates it because an enemy whose address you know is easier to watch than one without. Most agents do not know where the Open Ones get the names from; they believe the legend of the sale. ¶ What the Open Ones know about O.D.I.N.: Almost everything. The Opening, the Hands, the foster families, the Seeker, the Rule of the Twelve and why it exists. What they do not know: what the Council is called, where the Holds are, and who really stood behind the numbers that showed Kantor the hall in 1971.
- neu: What O.D.I.N. knows: That Kantor lives and leads the Open Ones. That they take children from foster families. Where their monastery lies, since 1976. The Council tolerates it, because an enemy whose address one knows is easier to observe than one without. Most agents do not know where the Open Ones get the names; they believe the legend of the sale. ¶ What the Open Ones know about O.D.I.N.: Almost everything. The Opening, the Hands, the foster families, the Seeker, the Rule of the Twelve and why it exists. What they do not know: what the Council is called, where the Holds lie, and who really stood behind the numbers that showed Kantor the hall in 1971.

- alt: One’s own name. The Open Ones offer to tell a psion of the cell what he was called before the Opening, who his parents are and whether they are still alive. The answer is on the list, and the Open Ones have it. ¶ In return they want little: that on one operation, once, he does not put on his helmet when they ask him to. He only learns why on that night.
- neu: One’s own name. The Open Ones offer to tell a Psion of the cell what they were called before the Opening, who their parents are and whether they are still alive. The answer lies on the list, and the Open Ones have it. ¶ In return they want little: that during one operation, just once, the Psion does not put on their helmet when the Open Ones ask. He only learns why on that night.

### 13 The Open Ones › Bases and Means

- alt: The monastery of Sfântul Ioan stands on a rocky ledge above a valley in the Eastern Carpathians, three storeys of stone, a bell tower without a bell. An old forest road leads up, impassable in winter, and a footpath through the beech wood. Twelve Open Ones and up to twenty children live in the monastery. The chapel is a dormitory, the refectory a classroom, in the cellar there are supplies for one winter. In Kantor’s room, on the windowsill, stands a photograph from 1952 showing a little girl in Leipzig. A psion without a helmet hears the monastery from a kilometre away, as if he were standing in front of a beehive. Whoever approaches is heard before being seen: Stealth does not help here, only a helmet or a Mental Barrier. ¶ The house in Hamburg-Altona is a terraced house in a quiet street near the Elbe. Downstairs lives a courier who works for Elster and passes for a quiet tenant. Upstairs lives an old Open One who no longer travels and never sleeps. In the cellar there are papers, cash and a crystal shard in a small lead box. Jonas Vogt works from here.

A Psion without a helmet hears the monastery from a kilometre away, as if standing before a beehive. Anyone approaching is heard before they are seen: Stealth does not help here, only a helmet or a Mental Barrier. ¶ The house in Hamburg-Altona is a terraced house on a quiet street near the Elbe. Downstairs lives a courier who works for Elster and is considered a quiet tenant. Upstairs lives an old Open One who no longer travels and never sleeps. In the cellar lie papers, cash and a crystal shard in a small lead box. Jonas Vogt works from here.

- alt: The locker at Zurich HB is on the lower level of the main station, row C. The Open Ones exchange messages, money and papers here, and sometimes there is a slip of paper with a name in it. Whoever opens the locker without a key finds it empty: a courier watches from a café opposite and empties it first as soon as a stranger stands in front of it too long.
- neu: The locker at Zurich HB lies in the basement of the main station, row C. The Open Ones exchange messages, money and papers here, and sometimes a note with a name lies inside. Anyone who opens the locker without a key finds it empty: a courier watches from a café opposite and empties it beforehand as soon as a stranger stands in front of it for too long.

- alt: Money: Little. The Open Ones live on Elster, on donations from former children and on what couriers earn. In the monastery there are twenty thousand euros in cash, plus an account in Zurich in the name of a foundation that supports orphanages in Romania. ¶ People: Twelve Open Ones in the monastery, a dozen more on the road in Europe, about thirty couriers and helpers, most of them children who were taken themselves. Three psions in the Choir, with helmets and on duty, pass messages on to the Open Ones. Who they are, only Kantor knows. ¶ Equipment and artefacts: No helmets, no focus devices, no alloy. Compact pistols, good papers, old cars. A Nexus crystal in the mill, a shard in Hamburg. Their strongest weapon is a piece of paper: the 1989 list, copied, added to and never complete. ¶ Connections: Elster and the Foundation in Basel. Children who are adults today and live all over Europe. Kantor hears the Singers when they sing, and he would hear a second Seeker if there were one anywhere. The Congregation knows that the Open Ones take children and has not yet decided whether that is a crime.
- neu: Money: Little. The Open Ones live off Elster, off donations from former children and off what couriers earn. In the monastery lie twenty thousand euros in cash, plus an account in Zurich in the name of a foundation that supports orphanages in Romania. ¶ People: Twelve Open Ones in the monastery, a dozen more travelling in Europe, about thirty couriers and helpers, most of them children they fetched themselves. Three Psions in the Choir, with helmets and on duty, pass messages to the Open Ones. Who they are, only Kantor knows. ¶ Equipment and artefacts: No helmets, no focus devices, no alloy. Compact pistols, good papers, old cars. One Nexus crystal in the mill, one shard in Hamburg. Their strongest weapon is a piece of paper: the list from 1989, copied, expanded and never complete. ¶ Connections: Elster and the Foundation in Basel. Children who are now adults and live all over Europe. Kantor hears the Singers when they sing, and he would hear a second Seeker if one existed anywhere. The Congregation knows that the Open Ones fetch children, and has not yet decided whether that is a crime.

### 13 The Open Ones › Hooks and Outlook

- alt: White File “The Mill”. A forester in the Vosges reports light in a mill that has stood empty for thirty years, and children’s voices by the stream. The Gendarmerie found nothing. The cell is to take a look. The mill is empty, the mattresses are still warm, and in the cellar something hums in a box of lead. ¶ Grey File “Foster Child”. A couple in Hamburg-Neugraben calls a number they should not even know, and an Open One is already on the way. See Grey Files, File 1. ¶ Red File “Row C”. The Echo operatives have found the locker in Zurich and copied a slip of paper with three names of foster families. The cell is to reach the three children before the Open Ones do. One of the names belongs to the family with whom a psion of the cell grew up. ¶ Black File “The Monastery”. The Council will no longer tolerate the monastery. The Echo operatives go in three nights from now, with orders to arrest Kantor and to “return” the children. The cell is to be there first, and no one tells it whether it should take Kantor, protect the children or only watch.
- neu: White File “The Mill”. A forester in the Vosges reports light in a mill that has stood empty for thirty years, and children’s voices by the stream. The Gendarmerie found nothing. The cell is to take a look. The mill is empty, the mattresses are still warm, and in the cellar something hums in a lead box. ¶ Grey File “Foster Child”. A married couple in Hamburg-Neugraben calls a number they should not know at all, and an Open One is already on the way. See Grey Files, File 1. ¶ Red File “Row C”. The Echo-Operatives have found the locker in Zurich and copied a note with three names of foster families. The cell is to reach the three children before the Open Ones do. One of the names belongs to the family in which a Psion of the cell grew up. ¶ Black File “The Monastery”. The Council no longer tolerates the monastery. The Echo-Operatives go in three nights from now, with orders to arrest Kantor and “return” the children. The cell is to be there beforehand, and no one tells them whether they should take Kantor, protect the children, or just watch.

- alt: On a winter night in the monastery of Sfântul Ioan, twelve Open Ones and twenty children sing, without helmets, in a circle on the floor of the old chapel. Kantor sits in the centre. All over Europe children hum the same melody in their sleep, and the Seeker hears the foreign choir louder than ever before. What answers then, no one knows. The game master decides it when the cell stands before it: perhaps nothing answers, and Kantor dies in the morning, an old man who waited too long. Perhaps a voice answers that Kantor calls Märta. Perhaps the Thirteenth answers, and in the morning the monastery is empty. ¶ The next plan: “The Answer” ○○○○○ . The Open Ones who are left want to understand what answered that night. They search for the children who hummed along in their sleep, and for that they need someone in the Choir who takes off his helmet.
- neu: After the Fifth Skull In one night in winter, twelve Open Ones and twenty children sing in the monastery of Sfântul Ioan, without helmets, in a circle on the floor of the old chapel. Kantor sits in the centre. All over Europe, children hum the same melody in their sleep, and the Seeker hears the alien choir louder than ever before. What answers then, no one knows. The game master decides it when the cell stands before it: perhaps nothing answers, and Kantor dies in the morning, an old man who waited too long. Perhaps a voice answers that calls Kantor Märta. Perhaps the Thirteenth answers, and the monastery is empty in the morning. ¶ The next plan: “The Answer” ○○○○○ . The Open Ones who remain want to understand what answered that night. They search for the children who hummed along in their sleep, and for that they need someone in the Choir who takes off their helmet.

- alt: File 1 “Foster Child” takes place while the plan stands at ☠☠☠○○ . If the cell rescues Linus, the plan loses a skull. If Jonas Vogt escapes with Linus, the plan gains a skull, and Linus is one of the children who sing in the monastery in the evening. If the cell persuades Vogt without a fight, Kantor remembers it: the next Bait goes to the psion who spoke with Vogt.
- neu: File 1 “Foster Child” takes place while the plan is at ☠☠☠○○ . If the cell saves Linus, the plan loses a skull. If Jonas Vogt escapes with Linus, the plan gains a skull, and Linus is one of the children who sing in the monastery in the evening. If the cell persuades Vogt without a fight, Kantor remembers it: the next Bait goes to the psion who spoke with Vogt.

- alt: In the campaign Kantor does not want to sing in the monastery but on the night of 14 February in Wales, in the hall above the third tunnel. Whoever plays the campaign follows it: the plan “Taking the Children” then only runs up to ☠☠☠☠○ , and what happens after that is decided by Mission 16 “The Monastery”.
- neu: In the campaign Kantor does not want to sing in the monastery but on the night of 14 February in Wales, in the hall above the third tunnel. Those who play the campaign follow it: the plan “Fetch the children” then runs only until ☠☠☠☠○ , and what happens after that is decided by Mission 16 “The Monastery”.

### 13 The Open Ones › The Plan: “Taking the Children”

- alt: Kantor wants to sing once more, and for that he needs voices that have never worn a helmet or have taken it off of their own free will. The Open Ones are taking more children than in the thirty years before, and they no longer take them only through the 1989 list but wherever Kantor hears them. The cell notices it in foster families who call a number instead of the police, in a man in a grey coat on a platform and in a humming that a psion of the cell has in his ear at night and does not recognise.
- neu: Kantor wants to sing once more, and for that he needs voices that have never worn a helmet or have taken it off of their own free will. The Open Ones are taking more children than in the thirty years before, and they no longer take them only through the 1989 list but wherever Kantor hears them. The cell notices it in foster families who call a number instead of the police, in a man in a grey coat on a platform, and in a humming that a Psion of the cell has in their ear at night and does not recognise.

- alt: ☠○○○○ Kantor hears the children himself. Couriers take the first ones who are not on the list from foster families in Romania and Hungary. Trail: A missing-child case in a local newspaper, never solved. The foster parents did not report the child missing. ¶ ☠☠○○○ The network stands: the mill in the Vosges, the house in Altona, the locker in Zurich. In Hamburg Jonas Vogt watches a child who hums at night. Trail: A Grey File emerges. A surveillance camera shows a man in a grey coat with the photo of a child in his hand. ¶ ☠☠☠○○ Within one month a child is missing one morning from three foster families. In the monastery the children sing together in the evening, first three, then seven. Trail: A psion of the cell wakes up and hums a melody he does not know. In the locker in Zurich lies a slip of paper with three names. ¶ ☠☠☠☠○ Kantor calls the Open Ones from all over Europe back to the monastery. Twelve children sing every evening without helmets, and Kantor waits for the last voices. Trail: A Red File emerges. The Seeker does not sleep for two nights and hears “a choir that is not ours”. ¶ ☠☠☠☠☠ The Open Ones have enough children to form a choir of their own, without helmets. They sing in the monastery of Sfântul Ioan. What answers then, no one knows. Trail: The Council takes over with a Black File. One night, all over Europe, children hum the same melody in their sleep. ¶ The Key Figure is Kantor. Without him no one sings: Ilinca Dragomir is afraid of what might answer, and the other Open Ones follow her. If Kantor is arrested, persuaded or weakened so much that he can no longer lead the monastery, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. Whoever wants to persuade him needs more than words: proof that Märta is not waiting, or proof that she is and that a choir without helmets does not reach her. The cell probably has neither. Whoever takes Kantor by force takes him out of a house full of children, and the cell never wins by harming a child. A cell that puts children in danger to get at Kantor does not take the skull away, it gives it to the plan.
- neu: ☠○○○○ Kantor hears the children himself. Couriers take the first ones who are not on the list from foster families in Romania and Hungary. A missing person case in a local newspaper, never solved. The foster parents did not report the child missing. ¶ ☠☠○○○ The network is in place: the mill in the Vosges, the house in Altona, the locker in Zurich. Jonas Vogt observes a child in Hamburg who hums at night. Trail: A Grey File emerges. A surveillance camera shows a man in a grey coat holding a photo of a child. ¶ ☠☠☠○○ In one month, a child is missing in the morning from three foster families. In the monastery the children sing together in the evening, first three, then seven. Sign: A Psion of the cell wakes up and hums a melody they do not know. In the locker in Zurich lies a note with three names. ¶ ☠☠☠☠○ Kantor calls the Open Ones from all over Europe back to the monastery. Twelve children sing every evening without a helmet, and Kantor waits for the last voices. Trail: A Red File emerges. The Seeker does not sleep for two nights and hears “a choir that is not ours”. ¶ ☠☠☠☠☠ The Open Ones have enough children to form their own choir, without helmets. They sing in the monastery of Sfântul Ioan. What answers then, no one knows. Trail: The Council takes over with a Black File. One night, all over Europe, children hum the same melody in their sleep. ¶ The Key Figure is Kantor. Without him no one sings: Ilinca Dragomir is afraid of what might answer, and the other Open Ones follow her. If Kantor is arrested, convinced, or weakened so much that he can no longer lead the monastery, the plan loses two skulls and does not roll after the next mission. Whoever wants to convince him needs more than words: proof that Märta is not waiting, or proof that she is and that a choir without helmets does not reach her. The cell probably has neither. Whoever takes Kantor by force takes him from a house full of children, and the cell never wins by harming a child. A cell that puts children in danger to get at Kantor does not take the skull away, it gives it to the plan.

- alt: If the cell takes a skull from the plan, Kantor answers with a countermove. He knows how O.D.I.N. thinks, because he helped build it.
- neu: If the cell takes a skull from the plan, Kantor answers with a countermove. He knows how O.D.I.N. thinks, for he helped build it.

- alt: 1 to 2 The Bait. A psion of the cell finds a slip of paper in his jacket pocket in a fine, old-fashioned hand: a first name, a place and a year. It is his own name, before the Print Shop gave him a new one. There is more if he answers. ¶ 3 to 4 The Voice in Sleep. Kantor seeks out a psion of the cell at night with Mental Whisper. At the start of the next mission the psion makes his Resonance roll, and his Observer asks why he is sleeping so badly. ¶ 5 to 6 The Family Moves. A foster family the cell has visited or is meant to protect is gone overnight, with the child and with papers from Elster’s workshop. Whoever wants to find them needs Contacts or Investigation against Very Hard (4).
- neu: 1 to 2 The Bait. A Psion of the cell finds in their jacket pocket a note in a fine, old-fashioned handwriting: a first name, a place, and a year. It is their own name, before the Print Shop gave them a new one. There is more if he answers. ¶ 3 to 4 The Voice in Sleep. Kantor seeks out a psion of the cell at night with Mental Whisper. The Psion makes their Resonance roll at the start of the next mission, and their Observer asks why they sleep so badly. ¶ 5 to 6 The Family Moves. A foster family the cell visited or is supposed to protect is gone overnight, with the child and with papers from Elster’s workshop. Whoever wants to find them needs Contacts or Investigation against Very Hard (4).

- alt: At ☠☠☠☠○ : “The Chimera” (Morrow Biologics) gains a skull. The Open Ones take a young woman with a sleeping gift out of the subject register of study MB-7 before she is enrolled, and the corporation speeds up its research in Lyon. ¶ At ☠☠☠☠○ : “Keep the Silence” (Congregation of the Silent Word) loses a skull. The Congregation sees that the Open Ones save children, and Don Matteo doubts whether his article is the right answer. ¶ At ☠☠☠☠☠ : “No More Nexus Crystals” (The Foundation in Basel) loses a skull. The Open Ones buy Nexus crystals for their choir themselves and take sources away from the Foundation.
- neu: At ☠☠☠☠○ : “The Chimera” (Morrow Biologics) gains a skull. The Open Ones take a young woman with a dormant gift from the subject register of study MB-7 before she is enrolled, and the corporation accelerates its research in Lyon. ¶ At ☠☠☠☠○ : “Preserve the silence” (Congregation of the Silent Word) loses a skull. The Congregation sees that the Open Ones save children, and Don Matteo doubts whether his article is the right answer. ¶ At ☠☠☠☠☠ : “No More Nexus Crystals” (The Foundation in Basel) loses a skull. The Open Ones buy Nexus crystals for their choir themselves and take sources from the Foundation.

## Kräfte Deutsch (2 Absätze)

### Schwerer Griff

- alt: Der Psion hebt eine Last bis zu einer Tonne: ein Auto, ein Tor, einen Stahlträger. Er bewegt sie 5 m pro Runde oder wirft sie einmal: Angriff gegen die Verteidigung, Schaden 5 + Überschuss, Durchschlag 1. Jede Runde nach der ersten kostet 1 Belastung.
- neu: Der Psion hebt eine Last bis zu einer Tonne: ein Auto, ein Tor, einen Stahlträger. Er bewegt sie 5 m pro Runde oder wirft sie einmal: Angriff mit den Erfolgen der Kraftprobe gegen die Verteidigung, Schaden 5 + Überschuss, Durchschlag 1. Jede Runde nach der ersten kostet 1 Belastung.

### Massenmanipulation

- alt: Bis zu drei Ziele führen einen einfachen Befehl aus, der sie nicht selbst gefährdet: „Lasst die Waffen fallen", „Lauft". Jedes Ziel leistet einzeln Widerstand. Der Psion erleidet danach 2 Belastung.
- neu: Bis zu drei Ziele führen einen einfachen Befehl aus, der sie nicht selbst gefährdet: „Lasst die Waffen fallen", „Lauft". Jedes Ziel leistet einzeln Widerstand. Der Psion erleidet 1 Belastung.

## Zauber Deutsch (2 Absätze)

### Wachpakt

- alt: Der Thaumaturg schließt einen Pakt mit einem Wesen, das einen Ort bis Sonnenaufgang bewacht. Es weckt ihn, sobald jemand den Ort betritt, und kämpft, wenn er es verlangt: Angriff 4 + 4, Schaden 4, Verteidigung 2, 12 LP. Der Pakt erzeugt eine Schuld (Regel „Schulden“, Klassenbuch Thaumaturg, Kapitel XI).
- neu: Der Thaumaturg schließt einen Pakt mit einem Wesen, das einen Ort bis Sonnenaufgang bewacht. Es weckt ihn, sobald jemand den Ort betritt, und kämpft, wenn er es verlangt: Angriff 4 + 4, Schaden 4, Verteidigung 2, 12 LP. Der Pakt erzeugt eine Schuld.

### Siegelschrift

- alt: Der Thaumaturg versiegelt eine Tür, eine Kiste oder ein Buch. Öffnen können sie nur er und bis zu drei Menschen, die er dabei nennt. Wer das Siegel mit Gewalt oder Magie bricht, braucht eine Probe gegen Sehr schwer (4), und der Thaumaturg spürt den Bruch, wo immer er ist.
- neu: Der Thaumaturg versiegelt eine Tür, eine Kiste oder ein Buch. Öffnen können sie nur er und bis zu drei Menschen, die er dabei nennt. Wer das Siegel mit Gewalt oder Magie bricht, braucht eine Probe gegen Sehr schwer (4), mit Gewalt auf ST + Athletik, mit Magie auf das Attribut des Zaubers und Thaumaturgie, und der Thaumaturg spürt den Bruch, wo immer er ist.

## Kräfte Englisch (6 Absätze)

### Deep Healing

- alt: Heals 5 + margin HP. A target can be healed this way at most once per day. The healer suffers 1 strain. Margin 2: The target also removes 1 strain.
- neu: Heals 5 + margin HP. A target can be healed this way at most once per day. The healer suffers 1 strain. Margin 2: The target also reduces 1 strain.

### Life Bond

- alt: The psion binds himself and up to three allies together. If one of them suffers damage, he may distribute it among the other bound characters as he chooses, in whole points. Anyone who leaves the bond or is incapacitated drops out. Unwilling targets cannot be bound. Afterwards 1 strain.
- neu: The psion binds himself and up to three allies together. If one of them suffers damage, they may distribute it among the other bound characters as they choose, in whole points. Anyone who leaves the bond or is incapacitated drops out. Unwilling targets cannot be bound. Afterwards 1 strain.

### Heavy Grip

- alt: The psion lifts a load of up to one tonne: a car, a gate, a steel girder. He moves it 5 m per round or throws it once: attack against defence, damage 5 + margin, penetration 1. Every round after the first costs 1 strain.
- neu: The psion lifts a load of up to one tonne: a car, a gate, a steel girder. They move it 5 m per round or throw it once: attack with the successes of the power test against defence, damage 5 + margin, Penetration 1. Every round after the first costs 1 strain.

### Silent Fortress

- alt: The psion shuts himself in. Until the end of the scene he does not have to make horror tests, and no power or spell can take over or read his mind. At the end of the scene everything catches up with him: he makes all missed horror tests in the same round.
- neu: The psion shuts himself in. Until the end of the scene they do not have to make horror tests, and no power and no spell can take over or read their mind. At the end of the scene everything catches up with them: they make all the missed horror tests in the same round.

### Rock Wall

- alt: A wall grows out of earth, stone or asphalt, up to 10 m long and 3 m high. It stops bullets and absorbs 15 damage before it is breached. Anyone standing where it rises is pushed aside. Not on wooden floors or upper storeys.
- neu: A wall grows out of earth, stone or tarmac, up to 10 m long and 3 m high. It stops bullets and absorbs 15 damage before a breach opens. Anyone standing where it rises is pushed aside. Not on wooden floors or on upper storeys.

### Inner Interrogation

- alt: The psion asks up to three questions, and the target answers truthfully in thought, as well as it knows the answer. Unwilling targets resist each question separately. The same target can be questioned this way at most once per day. Afterwards it remembers a conversation, not the questions. The psion suffers 1 strain.
- neu: The Psion asks up to three questions, and the target answers truthfully in thought, as far as they know the answer. Unwilling targets resist each question separately. The same target can be questioned this way at most once per day. Afterwards they remember a conversation, not the questions. The psion suffers 1 strain.

## Zauber Englisch (10 Absätze)

### Decomposition

- alt: An object up to the size of a door crumbles into dust and rust, or a target’s armour drops by 2 for the rest of the scene. Magical objects and artefacts are immune. The spell does not harm living beings.
- neu: An object up to the size of a door crumbles to dust and rust, or a target’s armour drops by 2 for the rest of the scene. Magical objects and artefacts are immune. The spell does not harm living beings.

### Lockstep

- alt: The Thaumaturge gives up to three allies their rhythm. They act before all opponents, regardless of initiative, and gain +1 defence. If Lockstep takes effect in a scene with Short Time Travel, Time Loop or Standstill (Chronomancer), the “Paradox” rule applies.
- neu: The Thaumaturge gives up to three allies their rhythm. They act before all opponents, regardless of initiative, and gain +1 defence. If Lockstep takes effect in a scene with Short Time Travel, Time Loop or Standstill (Chronomancer), the rule “Paradox” applies.

### The Creditor

- alt: The Thaumaturge summons a being they already owe something. It fights for them for three rounds: attack 5 + 5, damage 5, defence 3, 20 HP. Then it leaves and takes something. The pact creates a debt (“Debts” rule, Class Book Thaumaturge, chapter XI), and the Invoker suffers 2 strain.
- neu: The Thaumaturge calls a being to which they already owe something. It fights for them for three rounds: attack 5 + 5, damage 5, defence 3, 20 HP. Then it leaves and takes something. The pact creates a debt (“Debts” rule, Class Book Thaumaturge, chapter XI), and the Invoker suffers 2 strain.

### Doppelganger

- alt: Next to the Thaumaturge or an ally stands a second, perfect likeness. It speaks, walks and bleeds. Anyone attacking the original must first pass WIS + Perception against Hard (3), otherwise they hit the image, which then vanishes.
- neu: Next to the Thaumaturge or an ally stands a second, perfect likeness. It speaks, walks and bleeds. Anyone attacking the original must first pass WIS + Perception against Hard (3), or they hit the image, which then vanishes.

### Watch Pact

- alt: The Thaumaturge makes a pact with a being that guards a place until sunrise. It wakes them as soon as someone enters the place and fights if they demand it: attack 4 + 4, damage 4, defence 2, 12 HP. The pact creates a debt (“Debts” rule, Class Book Thaumaturge, chapter XI).
- neu: The Thaumaturge makes a pact with a being that guards a place until sunrise. It wakes them as soon as anyone enters the place, and fights if they demand it: attack 4 + 4, damage 4, defence 2, 12 HP. The pact creates a debt (“Debts” rule, Class Book Thaumaturge, chapter XI).

### The Lost Week

- alt: A person lives through a week of recovery in one minute: they heal 5 + margin HP, and injuries that would otherwise take days are healed. Afterwards they are a week older and very hungry. The Thaumaturge rolls on “Unequal Ageing”.
- neu: A person lives through a week of recovery in one minute: they heal 5 + margin HP, and injuries that would otherwise take days have healed. Afterwards they are a week older and very hungry. The Thaumaturge rolls for “Unequal Ageing”.

### The Great Circle

- alt: The Thaumaturge draws a warding circle around a building or a square. While it holds, no rift opens inside it, beings from rifts suffer 3 damage each round inside the circle, and spells and powers from outside into the circle are one step harder. Afterwards 2 strain.
- neu: The Thaumaturge draws a warding circle around a building or a square. As long as it holds, no rift opens inside it, beings from rifts suffer 3 damage each round they are in the circle, and spells and powers directed into the circle from outside are one level harder. Afterwards 2 strain.

### Blank Spot

- alt: All people in the area forget the last ten minutes. Unwilling targets resist, and the Thaumaturge can exclude allies. Recordings, notes and traces remain. Afterwards 2 strain.
- neu: All people in the area forget the last ten minutes. Unwilling targets resist, and the Thaumaturge can exempt allies. Recordings, notes and traces remain. Afterwards 2 strain.

### Red Tincture

- alt: One hour of work produces an elixir that lasts 24 hours. Whoever drinks it regains all HP and removes 3 strain. The Alchemist suffers 2 strain while brewing it.
- neu: An hour’s work produces an elixir that keeps for 24 hours. Whoever drinks it regains all their HP and reduces 3 strain. The Alchemist suffers 2 strain when brewing it.

### Seal Script

- alt: The Thaumaturge seals a door, a chest or a book. Only they and up to three people they name while casting can open it. Anyone who breaks the seal by force or magic needs a test against Very Hard (4), and the Thaumaturge feels the break wherever they are.
- neu: The Thaumaturge seals a door, a chest or a book. Only they and up to three people they name at the time can open it. Anyone who breaks the seal by force or magic needs a test against Very Hard (4), by force on STR + Athletics, by magic on the spell’s attribute and Thaumaturgy, and the Thaumaturge feels the breach wherever they are.
